Walkthrough

Whenever an Expected Part becomes fully identified — it gets both a brand and a part number, whether from the AI's Identify Parts job or from a user typing it in — the system now looks up or researches a resale-value estimate for it in the background, with no user action needed. Prices are cached by (category, brand, part number) and shared across every Listing, refreshed once a day, and — the trickiest part of this feature — never overwritten once a user has claimed that row's price as their own, even if they later clear it back to blank.

+/**
+ * Cache of an AI-researched resale-value estimate for a (category, brand,
+ * partNumber) combo (part-price-estimation feature), so a part identified a
+ * second time with the same combo reuses the prior research instead of
+ * re-querying the AI price-research module. `estimatedResaleValue` is null
+ * until the first successful research completes, or stays null forever for a
+ * combo research couldn't price; `lastResearchedAt` is null only in that same
+ * not-yet-researched state.
+ */
+export interface PartPriceCacheEntry {
+  id: string;
+  category: ComponentCategory;
+  brand: string;
+  partNumber: string;
+  estimatedResaleValue: number | null;
+  lastResearchedAt: string | null;
+  createdAt: string;
+  updatedAt: string;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Stage of one queued price-research job (part-price-estimation feature,
+ * assumption A5). Flat rather than multi-phase like
+ * `identify_parts_job_stage`: one price lookup is a single AI call, so the
+ * only states worth distinguishing are "waiting", "in a worker's hands",
+ * and the two terminal outcomes.
+ */
+export type PricePartsJobStage = 'queued' | 'pricing' | 'completed' | 'failed';
+
+/**
+ * One unit of background price research, keyed by the (category, brand,
+ * partNumber) combo rather than by an Expected Part or Listing id
+ * (assumption A1): Expected Part ids are recreated on every Listing save,
+ * and the researched value is Listing-independent anyway, so one job
+ * prices every row sharing the combo. `errorMessage` is set only on the
+ * `failed` stage; `startedAt`/`completedAt` are null until the job is
+ * claimed / reaches a terminal stage.
+ */
+export interface PricePartsJob {
+  id: string;
+  category: ComponentCategory;
+  brand: string;
+  partNumber: string;
+  stage: PricePartsJobStage;
+  errorMessage: string | null;
+  createdAt: string;
+  startedAt: string | null;
+  completedAt: string | null;
+}

The actual decision logic — given a freshly-saved batch of Expected Parts, which combos

Tasks: T4, T12, T13

  • the trigger itself — `triggerPriceLookups` de-dupes

    • @@ -0,0 +1,126 @@
      +// The central cache-hit/miss decision of the part-price-estimation feature —
+// nodes B through G of the feature's architecture diagram. Every write path
+// that creates Expected Parts (the identify-parts worker pool, and the
+// Listing create/update routes) hands its freshly-created rows here, and
+// this module decides, per distinct (category, brand, partNumber) combo,
+// which of three things happens:
+//
+//   B  a part is eligible at all — brand and partNumber both known, and no
+//      estimated resale value of its own yet (a part the user already priced
+//      is never touched, Req 14);
+//   C  look the combo up in part_price_cache;
+//   D  hit with a value present -> write that value straight onto every
+//      matching auto-owned Expected Part now (no AI call, no job: this is
+//      the whole point of the cache);
+//   E  hit with the value still null -> do nothing. The row's existence
+//      means some writer already took responsibility for researching this
+//      combo, so a second job here would be a duplicate;
+//   F  miss -> insert the cache row (race-safe: a concurrent writer may win,
+//      in which case insertPriceCacheEntry reports inserted: false rather
+//      than throwing);
+//   G  then enqueue a price_parts_job, unless one is already queued or
+//      pricing for this combo.
+//
+// The enqueue at G goes through the atomic createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent in
+// every path, not only when this writer won the insert race: the race
+// winner may not have reached its own enqueue yet when the loser looks, so
+// "I lost the insert" is not on its own evidence that a job exists.
+// Checking (and inserting into) the job table as one atomic statement is
+// what actually delivers Req 13's "never two duplicate jobs" — a separate
+// SELECT-then-INSERT pair left a window where two concurrent first
+// encounters of the same combo could both observe "no job yet" before
+// either insert landed.
+//
+// Nothing here is awaited by a request handler: price research is a
+// background queued job, never synchronous work inside the triggering
+// request (Req 5).
+import { logger } from '../logger.js';
+import {
+  createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent,
+  findPriceCacheEntry,
+  insertPriceCacheEntry,
+  propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts,
+  type ComponentCategory,
+  type ExpectedPart,
+} from '../db/index.js';
+
+/** One distinct combo to resolve, after eligibility filtering and de-duplication. */
+interface PriceCombo {
+  category: ComponentCategory;
+  brand: string;
+  partNumber: string;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Nodes C-G for one combo. Sequential by design: the cache read, the
+ * insert and the job check are three statements, and running the same combo
+ * through them twice concurrently is exactly the duplicate-job race the
+ * gating exists to prevent — which is why callers de-duplicate first and
+ * resolve combos one at a time.
+ */
+async function resolveCombo(combo: PriceCombo): Promise<void> {
+  const { category, brand, partNumber } = combo;
+  const cached = await findPriceCacheEntry(category, brand, partNumber);
+
+  if (cached !== null) {
+    // Node E: the combo is already known but unpriced — research is already
+    // someone's responsibility, so there is nothing to start here.
+    if (cached.estimatedResaleValue === null) {
+      return;
+    }
+    // Node D: a researched value is already on hand. Writing it now is what
+    // makes the second listing containing the same part priced instantly.
+    await propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts(category, brand, partNumber, cached.estimatedResaleValue);
+    return;
+  }
+
+  // Node F. The `inserted` flag is deliberately not used to gate the
+  // enqueue — see this file's header: losing the insert race says nothing
+  // about whether the winner has enqueued its job yet.
+  await insertPriceCacheEntry(category, brand, partNumber);
+
+  // Node G: atomic enqueue-unless-already-non-terminal, closing the race
+  // window a separate check-then-insert pair would leave open.
+  await createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent(category, brand, partNumber);
+}
+
+/**
+ * Runs the cache-hit/miss decision for every Expected Part in `parts` that
+ * is eligible for automatic pricing, enqueueing background research for the
+ * combos that need it and applying already-cached values to the rest.
+ *
+ * Combos are de-duplicated first: one Listing can easily hold two rows of
+ * the same part, and resolving the same combo twice would race against
+ * itself over the cache row and the job queue.
+ *
+ * A failure on one combo is logged and skipped rather than thrown: this is
+ * a best-effort background trigger hanging off a write that has already
+ * succeeded, and failing it must not fail (or abort) the write — the same
+ * per-item try/catch routes/listings.ts's promoteReferencesForExpectedParts
+ * already uses for the same reason.
+ */
+export async function triggerPriceLookups(expectedParts: ExpectedPart[]): Promise<void> {
+  const combos = new Map<string, PriceCombo>();
+  for (const part of expectedParts) {
+    // Node B: eligible only when fully identified (brand *and* partNumber —
+    // a half-identified part cannot be researched, and neither is ever
+    // guessed) and carrying no price of its own. A part that already has an
+    // estimated resale value is left alone, whoever put it there.
+    if (part.brand === null || part.partNumber === null || part.estimatedResaleValue !== null) {
+      continue;
+    }
+    combos.set(`${part.componentCategory}\u0000${part.brand}\u0000${part.partNumber}`, {
+      category: part.componentCategory,
+      brand: part.brand,
+      partNumber: part.partNumber,
+    });
+  }
+
+  for (const combo of combos.values()) {
+    try {
+      await resolveCombo(combo);
+    } catch (err) {
+      logger.error({ err, ...combo }, 'Failed to trigger price lookup for part combo');
+    }
+  }
+}

  • the cache table's CRUD, including the

    • @@ -0,0 +1,156 @@
      +// Typed CRUD for the `part_price_cache` table (part-price-estimation
+// feature T3). Caches an AI-researched resale-value estimate per
+// (component_category, brand, part_number) combo so a part identified a
+// second time with the same combo reuses the prior research instead of
+// re-querying the AI price-research module, and backs the daily refresh
+// sweep that re-prices every cached combo.
+import { getConnection } from '../client.js';
+import { newId } from '../id.js';
+import { toNullableNumber } from '../sqlHelpers.js';
+import type { ComponentCategory, PartPriceCacheEntry } from '../types.js';
+
+// Same pattern as referencePhotos.ts's / sourcingAttempts.ts's
+// UNIQUE_CONSTRAINT_MESSAGE: matches the DuckDB error text raised when the
+// unique index on (component_category, brand, part_number) is violated by a
+// second insert for the same combo.
+const UNIQUE_CONSTRAINT_MESSAGE = /constraint error.*violates unique constraint/i;
+
+// Converts one raw JSON row into the camelCase PartPriceCacheEntry shape.
+function mapRow(row: Record<string, unknown>): PartPriceCacheEntry {
+  return {
+    id: row.id as string,
+    category: row.component_category as ComponentCategory,
+    brand: row.brand as string,
+    partNumber: row.part_number as string,
+    estimatedResaleValue: toNullableNumber(row.estimated_resale_value as string | null),
+    lastResearchedAt: row.last_researched_at as string | null,
+    createdAt: row.created_at as string,
+    updatedAt: row.updated_at as string,
+  };
+}
+
+/**
+ * Fetches the cached price-research entry for one (category, brand,
+ * partNumber) combo, or null if that combo has never been queued for
+ * research.
+ */
+export async function findPriceCacheEntry(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+): Promise<PartPriceCacheEntry | null> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT * FROM part_price_cache
+     WHERE component_category = $category AND brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number`,
+    { category, brand, part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  const rows = reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+  return rows.length > 0 ? mapRow(rows[0]) : null;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Inserts a new, as-yet-unresearched cache row for a (category, brand,
+ * partNumber) combo (estimated_resale_value and last_researched_at both left
+ * null — the first successful research fills them in via
+ * updatePriceCacheEntryValue). Relies on the unique index on
+ * (component_category, brand, part_number) to make a concurrent double-
+ * insert for the identical combo impossible; a caught unique-constraint
+ * violation is treated as "already queued, skip" rather than thrown — same
+ * pattern as referencePhotos.ts's insertReferencePhotoRow and
+ * sourcingAttempts.ts's recordSourcingAttempt.
+ */
+export async function insertPriceCacheEntry(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+): Promise<{ inserted: boolean }> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  try {
+    await connection.runAndReadAll(
+      `INSERT INTO part_price_cache
+         (id, component_category, brand, part_number)
+       VALUES
+         ($id, $category, $brand, $part_number)`,
+      {
+        id: newId(),
+        category,
+        brand,
+        part_number: partNumber,
+      },
+    );
+    return { inserted: true };
+  } catch (err) {
+    if (err instanceof Error && UNIQUE_CONSTRAINT_MESSAGE.test(err.message)) {
+      return { inserted: false };
+    }
+    throw err;
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Records the outcome of researching one (category, brand, partNumber)
+ * combo. When `value` is a number, sets both `estimated_resale_value` and
+ * `last_researched_at` (a priced combo). When `value` is null (research ran
+ * but couldn't price the combo), only `last_researched_at` is touched —
+ * `estimated_resale_value` is deliberately left out of that SET clause so a
+ * later null-finding refresh can never regress a prior successful cached
+ * value back down to null.
+ */
+export async function updatePriceCacheEntryValue(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+  value: number | null,
+): Promise<void> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const setSql =
+    value === null
+      ? `last_researched_at = current_timestamp`
+      : `estimated_resale_value = $value, last_researched_at = current_timestamp`;
+  await connection.run(
+    `UPDATE part_price_cache
+     SET ${setSql}, updated_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE component_category = $category AND brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number`,
+    value === null
+      ? { category, brand, part_number: partNumber }
+      : { category, brand, part_number: partNumber, value },
+  );
+}
+
+/** Lists every cached price-research entry — feeds the daily refresh sweep. */
+export async function listAllPriceCacheEntries(): Promise<PartPriceCacheEntry[]> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(`SELECT * FROM part_price_cache`);
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson().map(mapRow);
+}
+
+/**
+ * Propagates a freshly cached (or refreshed) price onto every Expected Part
+ * sharing this exact (category, brand, partNumber) combo whose
+ * `price_source` is `'auto'`. Three-state semantics (assumption A22,
+ * correcting A8): null is *undecided* — nobody has claimed the price yet,
+ * so it's still eligible for the server's first auto-claim, not touched by
+ * this propagation; `'auto'` means the background pipeline currently owns
+ * the figure, which is exactly the state this UPDATE is allowed to
+ * overwrite; `'manual'` means the user has explicitly taken ownership (by
+ * typing a value, or by clearing one the pipeline had filled in), and must
+ * never be auto-overwritten again. Restricting this UPDATE's WHERE clause
+ * to `price_source = 'auto'` is what keeps a background cache refresh from
+ * ever touching a `'manual'` row.
+ */
+export async function propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+  value: number,
+): Promise<void> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  await connection.run(
+    `UPDATE expected_parts
+     SET estimated_resale_value = $value, updated_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE component_category = $category AND brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number
+       AND price_source = 'auto'`,
+    { category, brand, part_number: partNumber, value },
+  );
+}

  • the job queue's CRUD, including

    • @@ -0,0 +1,176 @@
      +// Typed CRUD for the `price_parts_jobs` table (part-price-estimation
+// feature). Backs the async price-research queue the price worker pool
+// drains: the trigger (jobs/pricePartsTrigger.ts) enqueues one job per
+// (category, brand, partNumber) combo that has no cached value yet and
+// returns immediately instead of blocking a request on the AI call, and the
+// pool uses the functions below to move each job through its stages.
+// Keyed by the combo rather than by an Expected Part or Listing id
+// (assumption A1): Expected Part ids are recreated on every Listing save,
+// and one researched value serves every row sharing the combo.
+import { getConnection } from '../client.js';
+import { newId } from '../id.js';
+import type { ComponentCategory, PricePartsJob, PricePartsJobStage } from '../types.js';
+
+// Converts one raw JSON row into the camelCase PricePartsJob shape.
+function mapRow(row: Record<string, unknown>): PricePartsJob {
+  return {
+    id: row.id as string,
+    category: row.component_category as ComponentCategory,
+    brand: row.brand as string,
+    partNumber: row.part_number as string,
+    stage: row.stage as PricePartsJobStage,
+    errorMessage: (row.error_message as string | null) ?? null,
+    createdAt: row.created_at as string,
+    startedAt: (row.started_at as string | null) ?? null,
+    completedAt: (row.completed_at as string | null) ?? null,
+  };
+}
+
+/** Enqueues a new `queued` price-research job for one (category, brand, partNumber) combo and returns the row as stored. */
+export async function createQueuedPriceJob(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+): Promise<PricePartsJob> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `INSERT INTO price_parts_jobs (id, component_category, brand, part_number, stage)
+     VALUES ($id, $category, $brand, $part_number, 'queued')
+     RETURNING *`,
+    { id: newId(), category, brand, part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return mapRow(reader.getRowObjectsJson()[0]);
+}
+
+/**
+ * Atomically enqueues a new `queued` price-research job for one (category,
+ * brand, partNumber) combo unless a job for that combo is already `queued`
+ * or `pricing` — the INSERT and the non-terminal-job check run as one
+ * statement (INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS), the same pattern
+ * referencePhotos.ts's insertReferencePhotoRow uses for its own
+ * nullable-part-number dedup case. This replaces the separate
+ * hasNonTerminalPriceJob-then-createQueuedPriceJob pair at the trigger's
+ * enqueue gate (Node G): two genuinely concurrent first-encounters of the
+ * same combo could both observe "no job yet" from the separate SELECT
+ * before either INSERT landed, producing two rows for one combo (Req 13).
+ * Folding the check into the INSERT's own WHERE clause closes that window
+ * — DuckDB evaluates the subquery and performs the insert as one atomic
+ * step, so a second concurrent caller for the same combo always sees the
+ * first caller's row (or lack of it) consistently. Returns whether a row
+ * was actually inserted, so callers can tell a real enqueue apart from a
+ * dedup-skip.
+ */
+export async function createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+): Promise<{ inserted: boolean }> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const result = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `INSERT INTO price_parts_jobs (id, component_category, brand, part_number, stage)
+     SELECT $id, $category, $brand, $part_number, 'queued'
+     WHERE NOT EXISTS (
+       SELECT 1 FROM price_parts_jobs
+       WHERE component_category = $category AND brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number
+         AND stage IN ('queued', 'pricing')
+     )`,
+    { id: newId(), category, brand, part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  const [{ Count: rowsAffected }] = result.getRowObjectsJson() as Array<{ Count: string }>;
+  return { inserted: Number(rowsAffected) > 0 };
+}
+
+/**
+ * Atomically claims the oldest `queued` job for a price worker to process:
+ * the subselect that picks the row and the UPDATE that flips its stage to
+ * `pricing` run as one statement, so two concurrent workers can never both
+ * claim the same job. Returns the claimed row, or null when nothing is
+ * queued. Same shape as identifyPartsJobs.ts's claimNextQueuedJob, for the
+ * same reason.
+ */
+export async function claimNextQueuedPriceJob(): Promise<PricePartsJob | null> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `UPDATE price_parts_jobs
+     SET stage = 'pricing', started_at = current_timestamp, updated_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE id = (
+       SELECT id FROM price_parts_jobs WHERE stage = 'queued' ORDER BY created_at LIMIT 1
+     )
+     RETURNING *`,
+  );
+  const rows = reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+  return rows.length > 0 ? mapRow(rows[0]) : null;
+}
+
+/** Sets a job's stage directly — the generic setter behind the recovery/transition paths. */
+export async function updatePriceJobStage(jobId: string, stage: PricePartsJobStage): Promise<void> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  await connection.run(
+    `UPDATE price_parts_jobs SET stage = $stage, updated_at = current_timestamp WHERE id = $id`,
+    { id: jobId, stage },
+  );
+}
+
+/** Marks a job `completed`. The researched value itself lives in part_price_cache, not on the job. */
+export async function completePriceJob(jobId: string): Promise<void> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  await connection.run(
+    `UPDATE price_parts_jobs
+     SET stage = 'completed', completed_at = current_timestamp, updated_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE id = $id`,
+    { id: jobId },
+  );
+}
+
+/** Marks a job `failed`, recording the error message for later diagnosis. */
+export async function failPriceJob(jobId: string, errorMessage: string): Promise<void> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  await connection.run(
+    `UPDATE price_parts_jobs
+     SET stage = 'failed', error_message = $error_message, completed_at = current_timestamp, updated_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE id = $id`,
+    { id: jobId, error_message: errorMessage },
+  );
+}
+
+/**
+ * True when a job for this combo is still `queued` or `pricing` — i.e. the
+ * combo already has research in flight. The trigger gates every enqueue on
+ * this being false, which is what keeps two writers racing on the same
+ * combo from queueing two duplicate jobs (Req 13).
+ */
+export async function hasNonTerminalPriceJob(
+  category: ComponentCategory,
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+): Promise<boolean> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT 1 FROM price_parts_jobs
+     WHERE component_category = $category AND brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number
+       AND stage IN ('queued', 'pricing')
+     LIMIT 1`,
+    { category, brand, part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson().length > 0;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Resets every job stuck in the non-terminal, in-progress `pricing` stage
+ * back to `queued`, clearing `started_at`. Run once at pool startup, same
+ * reasoning as identifyPartsJobs.ts's own resetOrphanedJobsToQueued: a job
+ * left in `pricing` means the process crashed or was restarted mid-call,
+ * since a worker always moves a claimed job to a terminal stage
+ * (`completed`/`failed`) before picking up the next one. Returns the ids
+ * of every job reset, for startup logging.
+ */
+export async function resetOrphanedPriceJobsToQueued(): Promise<string[]> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `UPDATE price_parts_jobs
+     SET stage = 'queued', started_at = NULL, updated_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE stage = 'pricing'
+     RETURNING id`,
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson().map((row) => row.id as string);
+}

+/**
+ * Creates every Expected Part of a validated create/update body on
+ * `listingId`, deciding each row's `priceSource` on the way in, and returns
+ * the rows as stored (which the price trigger then works from).
+ *
+ * `priceSource` is three-state (assumption A22, correcting A8): null is
+ * *undecided*, `'auto'` means the background pipeline owns the row's price,
+ * `'manual'` means the user does (he typed a value, or cleared one the
+ * pipeline had filled in). Only an undecided row is ever claimed here, and
+ * only when it is fully identified (brand *and* partNumber) with no price
+ * of its own; an incoming `'auto'`/`'manual'` is stored verbatim. That is
+ * what makes Req 14/15 hold across a resave: on the old two-value field a
+ * cleared auto-priced row was indistinguishable from an undecided one, so
+ * it got re-claimed and then re-filled from the cache in the same request.
+ */
+async function createExpectedPartsForListing(
+  listingId: string,
+  expectedParts: ExpectedPartInput[],
+): Promise<ExpectedPart[]> {
+  return Promise.all(
+    expectedParts.map((part) =>
+      createExpectedPart({
+        listingId,
+        ...part,
+        priceSource:
+          (part.priceSource ?? null) === null &&
+          part.brand !== null &&
+          part.partNumber !== null &&
+          part.estimatedResaleValue === null
+            ? 'auto'
+            : part.priceSource ?? null,
+      }),
+    ),
+  );
+}

The actual research call — the prompt that asks for one honest, used-condition price

Tasks: T2, T5

  • builds the research prompt, reusing

    • @@ -0,0 +1,54 @@
      +// Builds the exact prompt text sent to the model for estimating one
+// specific part's used resale value (part-price-estimation feature T2).
+// Split out, same reasoning as imageSourcingPrompt.ts/catalogSourcingPrompt.ts:
+// the wording is the part most likely to be tuned, kept reviewable and
+// unit-testable without the process-spawning machinery around it.
+import type { ComponentCategory } from '@pc-parts-flipper/shared';
+
+/**
+ * Hard ceiling on one price-estimation call. Tools stay enabled (the model
+ * is expected to browse for recent used-market comparables, not just recall
+ * a number from training data), so this needs real round-trip room — but
+ * unlike catalog sourcing's DEFAULT_OMP_CATALOG_SOURCING_TIMEOUT_MS (10
+ * minutes, an unbounded whole-category crawl) this call targets one exact
+ * part, closer in shape to single-product image sourcing's
+ * DEFAULT_OMP_SOURCING_TIMEOUT_MS (5 minutes). Set to 3 minutes: enough for
+ * a few real searches on one specific part without letting a wedged call pin
+ * a request open indefinitely.
+ */
+export const DEFAULT_PRICE_ESTIMATION_TIMEOUT_MS = 180_000;
+
+/**
+ * Builds the prompt for estimating one part's used resale value. Instructs
+ * the model to research the exact category/brand/part number given — never
+ * a close substitute — and report a single USD figure for a used,
+ * working/good-condition unit, based on real current market activity it can
+ * find rather than a recalled list price. Mirrors
+ * identificationPrompt.ts's own honesty/conservatism instruction (rule 4
+ * there): told plainly to say so rather than guess when it cannot support a
+ * number, the model reports "not confident" instead of inventing a
+ * plausible-looking figure that would silently become a real asking price
+ * downstream. Do not weaken that instruction.
+ */
+export function buildPriceEstimationPrompt(category: ComponentCategory, brand: string, partNumber: string): string {
+  return [
+    `Estimate the current used resale price of the exact component specified below.`,
+    '',
+    `COMPONENT DETAILS:`,
+    `Category: ${category}`,
+    `Brand: ${brand}`,
+    `Part Number: ${partNumber}`,
+    '',
+    `INSTRUCTIONS:`,
+    '1. Research the actual current resale market for this exact part — recent sold listings, marketplace asking prices, or other real current activity. Do NOT substitute a similar or newer part, and do NOT just recall a manufacturer list price from training data.',
+    '2. Estimate what this exact part, in used, working/good condition (not new, not broken-for-parts), would sell for in US dollars right now.',
+    '3. Report one single price estimate, not a range.',
+    '4. Be honest and conservative. If you cannot find enough real market activity for this exact part to support a confident number, say so plainly — set "confident" to false — rather than guessing. An honest "not confident" is far more useful than a confident wrong price.',
+    '5. Do NOT invent, round-trip-guess, or pad a number you cannot actually support from what you found.',
+    '',
+    'RESPOND WITH ONLY RAW JSON. No markdown, no code fences, no commentary before or after:',
+    '{"confident":true,"priceUsd":45.00}',
+    'OR, if you cannot confidently estimate a price:',
+    '{"confident":false,"priceUsd":null}',
+  ].join('\n');
+}

  • `parsePriceEstimationResponse` — only a structurally

    • @@ -0,0 +1,69 @@
      +// Parses the model's raw stdout into a single confident price, or null
+// (part-price-estimation feature T2). Unlike identificationParser.ts and
+// catalogSourcingParser.ts, structural failure here is NOT fatal: this
+// module's whole contract is "say so rather than guess" (see
+// priceEstimationPrompt.ts's rule 4), so a refusal, a price range, "no
+// price found", unparseable prose, or any other shape the model returns
+// instead of a clean confident number is exactly as valid an answer as an
+// explicit confident:false — all of them fall through to null rather than
+// throwing. The only thing that ever produces a non-null result is a
+// structurally valid {"confident":true,"priceUsd":<finite positive number>}
+// response; every other case is logged at Warn (never Error — an honest "I
+// don't know" is not a failure) and returns null. Never invents a number.
+import { logger } from '../logger.js';
+import { stripCodeFence } from './identificationParser.js';
+
+// JSON.parse throws a SyntaxError whose message leaks nothing useful here;
+// caught and treated as "no confident price" rather than rethrown, since
+// non-JSON prose (a plain-English refusal) is an expected, valid shape for
+// this endpoint to receive, not a failure to restate as a 502.
+function parseJson(text: string): unknown {
+  try {
+    return JSON.parse(text);
+  } catch {
+    return undefined;
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Parses the runner's raw stdout into a single confident used-resale price
+ * estimate, or null when the model was not confident, returned a range,
+ * answered in unparseable prose, or produced any other shape besides a
+ * clean {"confident":true,"priceUsd":<number>}. Never throws, and never
+ * returns an invented/guessed number: anything short of a structurally
+ * valid, strictly positive, finite `priceUsd` paired with `confident:true`
+ * is treated as "no confident price" and normalized to null.
+ */
+export function parsePriceEstimationResponse(rawStdout: string): number | null {
+  const text = stripCodeFence(rawStdout);
+  const parsed = parseJson(text);
+
+  if (typeof parsed !== 'object' || parsed === null) {
+    logger.warn({ outputPreview: text.slice(0, 200) }, 'AI price estimation output was not valid JSON; treating as no confident price');
+    return null;
+  }
+
+  // `in` narrowing rather than an inline cast: the parsed value is untrusted
+  // model output, so the property is read as `unknown` and confirmed below.
+  const confident: unknown = 'confident' in parsed ? parsed.confident : undefined;
+  if (confident !== true) {
+    // A model that honestly reports confident:false (or omits/misshapes the
+    // field) is behaving correctly per the prompt's own instruction — no
+    // warning needed, this is the expected "I don't know" path.
+    return null;
+  }
+
+  const priceUsd: unknown = 'priceUsd' in parsed ? parsed.priceUsd : undefined;
+  if (typeof priceUsd === 'number' && Number.isFinite(priceUsd) && priceUsd > 0) {
+    return priceUsd;
+  }
+
+  // confident=true but priceUsd is malformed (missing, not a number,
+  // non-finite, zero/negative) is a failure to deliver what was asked for —
+  // coerce to null and log it, never trust an unverified number through.
+  logger.warn(
+    { priceUsd },
+    'AI price estimation returned confident=true but with a malformed priceUsd field; treating as no confident price',
+  );
+  return null;
+}

  • the worker pool itself — claim/pump/process, mirrors

    • @@ -0,0 +1,140 @@
      +// Manages an async worker pool that claims queued price-research jobs and
+// processes them to completion (or failure). Jobs transition through
+// stages as they run: 'queued' → 'pricing' (via claim), then to a terminal
+// stage. Unlike identify-parts jobs, a price job has only one terminal
+// *success* shape to distinguish from failure, not several intermediate
+// stages (see types.ts's PricePartsJobStage doc) — "no confident price" is
+// itself a successful, completed outcome (Req 11), not a failure, so
+// completed vs failed here tracks "did the AI call itself run" rather than
+// "did it find a price". The pool size is configurable via
+// PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE (default 1) so the number of concurrent AI pricing
+// passes can be tuned for available resources, same convention as
+// identifyPartsWorkerPool.ts's own IDENTIFY_PARTS_POOL_SIZE.
+// On startup, any jobs left in the 'pricing' stage (e.g., from a previous
+// server crash) are reset to 'queued' so they re-enter the queue and are
+// re-processed by the new pool instance.
+import { logger } from '../logger.js';
+import {
+  claimNextQueuedPriceJob,
+  completePriceJob,
+  failPriceJob,
+  propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts,
+  resetOrphanedPriceJobsToQueued,
+  updatePriceCacheEntryValue,
+} from '../db/index.js';
+import type { PricePartsJob } from '../db/index.js';
+import { buildPriceEstimationPrompt, DEFAULT_PRICE_ESTIMATION_TIMEOUT_MS } from '../ai/priceEstimationPrompt.js';
+import { parsePriceEstimationResponse } from '../ai/priceEstimationParser.js';
+import { getOmpSourcingRunner } from '../ai/ompSourcingRunner.js';
+
+/**
+ * Maximum number of price-research jobs to process concurrently, parsed
+ * from PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE. A missing, empty, or non-numeric value falls
+ * back to the default of 1 — same `parsePoolSize` convention as
+ * identifyPartsWorkerPool.ts's own fix: `Number(raw ?? 1)` alone only
+ * catches a strictly-nullish env var, since an empty string parses to 0
+ * and a non-numeric string parses to NaN, and either one makes pump()'s
+ * `activeCount < POOL_SIZE` always false, so jobs enqueue but are silently
+ * never processed. 0 itself is a valid, intentional value (disables the
+ * pool entirely so the real `omp` binary is never invoked in tests), so
+ * only a negative or non-finite parse falls back, not 0.
+ */
+function parsePoolSize(raw: string | undefined): number {
+  if (raw === undefined || raw.trim() === '') return 1;
+  const parsed = Number(raw);
+  return Number.isFinite(parsed) && parsed >= 0 ? parsed : 1;
+}
+
+const POOL_SIZE = parsePoolSize(process.env.PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE);
+
+/** Number of jobs currently being processed. */
+let activeCount = 0;
+
+/**
+ * Attempts to claim and process queued price-research jobs up to the pool
+ * size. Called once at startup, once per job completion (to immediately
+ * refill the freed slot), and once per poll interval tick as a safety net
+ * in case a claim silently stalled.
+ */
+async function pump(): Promise<void> {
+  while (activeCount < POOL_SIZE) {
+    const job = await claimNextQueuedPriceJob();
+    if (job === null) {
+      break;
+    }
+    activeCount += 1;
+    // Fire off processJob without awaiting. The job runs to completion or
+    // rejection, then decrements activeCount and calls pump again.
+    void processJob(job);
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Processes a single price-research job: builds the estimation prompt for
+ * its (category, brand, partNumber) combo, runs it through the
+ * tools-enabled `omp` sourcing runner (no photo attached — the model
+ * browses for comparables itself), and parses the raw output into a
+ * confident price or null. Both outcomes — a number or null — are a
+ * successful completion (Req 11): "no confident price" is the AI's own
+ * honest answer, not a pipeline failure, so it is cached and the job is
+ * completed exactly like a found price. The cache is updated first in
+ * either case; propagation onto matching auto-managed Expected Parts only
+ * happens for a non-null value, since null has nothing to push.
+ * `failPriceJob` is reserved for a genuine runner failure — a spawn error,
+ * a timeout, or any other rejection from getOmpSourcingRunner() itself —
+ * mirroring identifyPartsWorkerPool.ts's own completed-vs-failed split
+ * between "the pipeline ran and returned a result" and "the pipeline
+ * itself threw".
+ */
+async function processJob(job: PricePartsJob): Promise<void> {
+  try {
+    const prompt = buildPriceEstimationPrompt(job.category, job.brand, job.partNumber);
+    const rawStdout = await getOmpSourcingRunner()(prompt, { timeoutMs: DEFAULT_PRICE_ESTIMATION_TIMEOUT_MS });
+    const value = parsePriceEstimationResponse(rawStdout);
+
+    await updatePriceCacheEntryValue(job.category, job.brand, job.partNumber, value);
+    if (value !== null) {
+      await propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts(job.category, job.brand, job.partNumber, value);
+    }
+    await completePriceJob(job.id);
+  } catch (err) {
+    // Derive error message the same way other error paths in this codebase
+    // do, so the message is always a string.
+    const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
+    await failPriceJob(job.id, message);
+  } finally {
+    activeCount -= 1;
+    // Fire off pump without awaiting; it continues its background loop.
+    void pump();
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Starts the price-research worker pool: resets any jobs left in the
+ * 'pricing' stage (a prior crash) back to 'queued', logs the reset count,
+ * pumps once to start background work, and sets up a 1000ms polling
+ * interval to periodically retry claiming work. Returns a handle with a
+ * stop() method that clears the interval, mirroring
+ * identifyPartsWorkerPool.ts's own startIdentifyPartsWorkerPool.
+ */
+export function startPricePartsWorkerPool(): { stop(): void } {
+  // Recover any jobs left in the 'pricing' stage from a prior crash.
+  void (async () => {
+    const resetJobIds = await resetOrphanedPriceJobsToQueued();
+    logger.info({ resetCount: resetJobIds.length }, 'Recovered orphaned price-parts jobs');
+  })();
+
+  // Pump once synchronously (without awaiting) to start background work.
+  void pump();
+
+  // Poll periodically to claim work. The interval runs pump every 1000ms.
+  const intervalHandle = setInterval(() => {
+    void pump();
+  }, 1000);
+
+  return {
+    stop(): void {
+      clearInterval(intervalHandle);
+    },
+  };
+}

The once-a-day sweep that re-researches every cached combo regardless of whether it

Tasks: T6, T7, T14

  • `runScheduledPriceRefresh`, cron'd for 3am (one

    • @@ -0,0 +1,98 @@
      +// Registers and runs the once-daily automated price-refresh sweep (Req 8,
+// part-price-estimation feature): walks every (category, brand,
+// partNumber) combo already sitting in part_price_cache and re-queues a
+// price_parts_job for each one that doesn't already have a non-terminal job
+// in flight, so T5's worker pool re-researches every cached combo — whether
+// or not it currently has a price — without a user having to trigger it.
+// runScheduledPriceRefresh is exported separately (rather than inlined as
+// the cron callback) so it can be unit-tested directly without waiting on a
+// real cron tick (test/priceRefreshScheduler.test.ts). Fires at 3am, one
+// hour after scan/scheduler.ts's own 2am scan job, so the two background
+// jobs never compete for AI/web-call resources at the same time
+// (assumption A4).
+import { schedule, type ScheduledTask } from 'node-cron';
+import { logger } from '../logger.js';
+import {
+  createQueuedPriceJob,
+  hasNonTerminalPriceJob,
+  listAllPriceCacheEntries,
+} from '../db/index.js';
+
+// Once daily at 3am local time — low-traffic hours, and distinct from
+// scan/scheduler.ts's 2am scan job so the two never run concurrently.
+const SCHEDULE_EXPRESSION = '0 3 * * *';
+
+/**
+ * Runs one scheduled price-refresh pass across every (category, brand,
+ * partNumber) combo currently in part_price_cache (Req 8). For each combo,
+ * enqueues a new price_parts_job only when no non-terminal (queued or
+ * pricing) job already exists for that combo (T4's hasNonTerminalPriceJob
+ * dedup check) — this sweep must never pile up duplicate jobs behind one
+ * already in flight. Every cache entry is swept regardless of whether it
+ * currently holds a price, since the point of the sweep is to refresh
+ * stale values, not merely to backfill missing ones. Logs an Info line when
+ * the run starts and an Info summary (how many combos were enqueued,
+ * skipped as already in flight, or errored) when it completes. Each
+ * entry's own dedup-check-and-enqueue is isolated in its own try/catch —
+ * mirroring scanService.ts's scanAllEligibleSources — so a transient
+ * failure on one combo (a DB error, for instance) is logged by combo and
+ * skipped rather than throwing once and silently aborting every remaining
+ * entry for the night (Req 8: every combo must still be re-researched).
+ * The outer try/catch remains to guard listAllPriceCacheEntries itself and
+ * any other failure outside the per-entry loop from crashing the cron
+ * callback / the server process.
+ */
+export async function runScheduledPriceRefresh(): Promise<void> {
+  logger.info('Scheduled price refresh starting');
+  try {
+    const entries = await listAllPriceCacheEntries();
+
+    let enqueuedCount = 0;
+    let skippedCount = 0;
+    let erroredCount = 0;
+    for (const entry of entries) {
+      try {
+        const alreadyQueued = await hasNonTerminalPriceJob(entry.category, entry.brand, entry.partNumber);
+        if (alreadyQueued) {
+          skippedCount += 1;
+          continue;
+        }
+        await createQueuedPriceJob(entry.category, entry.brand, entry.partNumber);
+        enqueuedCount += 1;
+      } catch (err) {
+        // One combo's dedup-check or enqueue failing must never block the
+        // rest of the sweep (Req 8); log which combo was skipped and move
+        // on to the next entry.
+        const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
+        erroredCount += 1;
+        logger.error(
+          { category: entry.category, brand: entry.brand, partNumber: entry.partNumber, err: message },
+          'Scheduled price refresh failed for one cache entry; continuing with the rest',
+        );
+      }
+    }
+
+    logger.info(
+      { enqueuedCount, skippedCount, erroredCount, totalCount: entries.length },
+      'Scheduled price refresh completed',
+    );
+  } catch (err) {
+    // A rejection outside the per-entry loop (e.g. listAllPriceCacheEntries
+    // itself failing) must still never crash the process.
+    const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
+    logger.error({ err: message }, 'Scheduled price refresh run failed unexpectedly');
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Registers the once-daily scheduled price-refresh job (Req 8) via
+ * node-cron and returns the resulting ScheduledTask handle. Called once
+ * during server bootstrap (index.ts); the cron job then lives for the
+ * lifetime of the process, firing runScheduledPriceRefresh() on each tick.
+ */
+export function startPriceRefreshScheduler(): ScheduledTask {
+  logger.info({ schedule: SCHEDULE_EXPRESSION }, 'Registering scheduled price refresh job');
+  return schedule(SCHEDULE_EXPRESSION, () => {
+    void runScheduledPriceRefresh();
+  });
+}

+    expect(fetchListingMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+
+    // t=4000ms: the pricing-poll loop's own 2s interval fires independently
+    // of the (already-cleared) job-stage poll and re-fetches the Listing.
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    expect(fetchListingMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);
+    expect(fetchListingMock).toHaveBeenNthCalledWith(2, 'listing-1');
+    expect(findPricingStatus(el)?.textContent).toBe('Pricing 1 part(s)…');
+  });
+
+  it('clears the pricing status, and stops polling, once a tick finds zero parts still unpriced', async () => {
+    fetchLatestIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(null);
+    enqueueIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(makeQueuedJob());
+    fetchIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(makeCompletedJob(twoPartResponse.parts, ''));
+    fetchListingMock
+      .mockResolvedValueOnce(listingWithUnpricedPart) // applyTerminalJob's own fetch: still unpriced
+      .mockResolvedValueOnce(listingWithPricedPart); // first pricing-poll tick: the price arrived
+
+    const el = await mountForm(savedListing);
+    await act(async () => findIdentifyButton(el)!.click());
+
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    expect(findPricingStatus(el)?.textContent).toBe('Pricing 1 part(s)…');
+
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    expect(findPricingStatus(el)).toBeUndefined();
+
+    // The interval was cleared, not merely quiet this tick: a third 2s
+    // produces no further fetchListing call.
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    expect(fetchListingMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);
+  });
+
+  it('gives up silently after 60 polls (2 minutes) if a part never resolves a price, with no error banner', async () => {
+    fetchLatestIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(null);
+    enqueueIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(makeQueuedJob());
+    fetchIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(makeCompletedJob(twoPartResponse.parts, ''));
+    fetchListingMock.mockResolvedValue(listingWithUnpricedPart); // every call: still unpriced
+
+    const el = await mountForm(savedListing);
+    await act(async () => findIdentifyButton(el)!.click());
+
+    // t=2000ms: job completes and starts the 60-poll pricing loop.
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    expect(findPricingStatus(el)?.textContent).toBe('Pricing 1 part(s)…');
+
+    // 60 pricing-poll ticks, each still finding the part unpriced.
+    for (let i = 0; i < 60; i += 1) {
+      await act(async () => {
+        await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+      });
+    }
+
+    expect(findPricingStatus(el)).toBeUndefined();
+    expect(el.querySelector('[role="alert"]')).toBeNull();
+    // 1 (applyTerminalJob) + 60 (exhausted polls) fetches, then the
+    // interval is gone -- a 61st tick produces no further call.
+    expect(fetchListingMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(61);
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    expect(fetchListingMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(61);
+  });
+
+  it('updates the displayed estimated resale value via onPartsSaved on each pricing-poll tick, not just the status text (A16)', async () => {
+    fetchLatestIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(null);
+    enqueueIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(makeQueuedJob());
+    fetchIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(makeCompletedJob(twoPartResponse.parts, ''));
+    fetchListingMock
+      .mockResolvedValueOnce(listingWithUnpricedPart)
+      .mockResolvedValueOnce(listingWithPricedPart);
+
+    const el = await mountForm(savedListing);
+    await act(async () => findIdentifyButton(el)!.click());
+
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    const beforePoll = Array.from(el.querySelectorAll('input[type="number"]')) as HTMLInputElement[];
+    expect(beforePoll[1].value).toBe('');
+
+    await act(async () => {
+      await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(2000);
+    });
+    const afterPoll = Array.from(el.querySelectorAll('input[type="number"]')) as HTMLInputElement[];
+    expect(afterPoll[1].value).toBe('275');
+  });
+});
+
+describe('Identify Parts price-research status — manual save never shows one (Req 17)', () => {
+  it('shows no pricing status for a Listing saved without ever clicking Identify Parts', async () => {
+    fetchLatestIdentifyPartsJobMock.mockResolvedValue(null);
+    const onSubmit = vi.fn(async () => savedListing);
+    const el = await mount(<ListingForm initialValue={savedListing} sources={sources} onSubmit={onSubmit} onCancel={() => {}} onSaved={() => {}} />);
+
+    const form = el.querySelector('form') as HTMLFormElement;
+    await act(async () => form.requestSubmit());
+
+    expect(onSubmit).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(findPricingStatus(el)).toBeUndefined();
+    // Not merely absent right now: Identify Parts was never clicked, so
+    // startPricingPolling (only ever reachable from applyTerminalJob) was
+    // never invoked at all.
+    expect(enqueueIdentifyPartsJobMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+});

      +// Tests for price estimation prompt builder and response parser
+// (part-price-estimation feature T2).
+import { describe, it, expect, beforeAll, afterEach, vi } from 'vitest';
+import type * as PromptModule from '../src/ai/priceEstimationPrompt.js';
+import type * as ParserModule from '../src/ai/priceEstimationParser.js';
+import type * as LoggerModule from '../src/logger.js';
+
+let prompt: typeof PromptModule;
+let parser: typeof ParserModule;
+let logger: typeof LoggerModule.logger;
+
+beforeAll(async () => {
+  // Dynamic imports so test setup can mock logger before importing modules
+  const modules = await Promise.all([
+    import('../src/ai/priceEstimationPrompt.js'),
+    import('../src/ai/priceEstimationParser.js'),
+    import('../src/logger.js'),
+  ]);
+
+  prompt = modules[0] as typeof PromptModule;
+  parser = modules[1] as typeof ParserModule;
+  logger = (modules[2] as typeof LoggerModule).logger;
+});
+
+afterEach(() => {
+  vi.restoreAllMocks();
+});
+
+describe('buildPriceEstimationPrompt', () => {
+  it('includes the category, brand, and partNumber verbatim', () => {
+    const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3060');
+
+    expect(text).toContain('GPU');
+    expect(text).toContain('NVIDIA');
+    expect(text).toContain('RTX 3060');
+  });
+
+  it('asks for a used, working/good-condition estimate rather than new price', () => {
+    const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt('CPU', 'Intel', 'i7-13700K');
+
+    expect(text.toLowerCase()).toContain('used');
+    expect(text.toLowerCase()).toContain('working');
+  });
+
+  it('instructs the model to say so rather than guess when not confident (rule 4)', () => {
+    const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt('RAM', 'Corsair', 'CMK16GX4M2B3200C16');
+
+    // Match rule 4's own sentence specifically via phrases unique to it —
+    // not just 'confident' (also appears in the JSON output examples) or
+    // 'guess' (also appears in rule 5's 'round-trip-guess') — so deleting
+    // rule 4 actually fails this test.
+    expect(text).toContain('say so plainly');
+    expect(text).toContain('rather than guessing');
+  });
+
+  it('instructs raw-JSON-only output with both confident and priceUsd keys', () => {
+    const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt('Storage', 'Samsung', '970 EVO Plus 1TB');
+
+    expect(text).toContain('RAW JSON');
+    expect(text).toContain('No markdown, no code fences');
+    expect(text).toContain('"confident"');
+    expect(text).toContain('"priceUsd"');
+  });
+
+  it('asks for a single price, explicitly not a range', () => {
+    const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt('PSU', 'EVGA', 'SuperNOVA 750 G5');
+
+    expect(text.toLowerCase()).toContain('single');
+    expect(text.toLowerCase()).toContain('not a range');
+  });
+
+  it('instructs researching the exact part rather than substituting a similar or newer one (Req 2: that specific category+brand+partNumber)', () => {
+    const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt('Motherboard', 'ASUS', 'ROG STRIX B550-F');
+
+    expect(text.toLowerCase()).toContain('this exact part');
+    expect(text.toLowerCase()).toContain('do not substitute a similar or newer part');
+  });
+
+  it('works with all component categories', () => {
+    const categories = ['CPU', 'GPU', 'Motherboard', 'RAM', 'Storage', 'PSU', 'Case', 'Cooler', 'Other'] as const;
+
+    for (const category of categories) {
+      const text = prompt.buildPriceEstimationPrompt(category, 'TestBrand', 'TestPart');
+      expect(text).toContain(category);
+      expect(text).toContain('TestBrand');
+      expect(text).toContain('TestPart');
+    }
+  });
+});
+
+describe('parsePriceEstimationResponse', () => {
+  it('parses a valid confident:true response to its priceUsd number', () => {
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: true, priceUsd: 189.5 });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBe(189.5);
+  });
+
+  it('returns null for an explicit confident:false response, without warning', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: false, priceUsd: null });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    // An honest "I don't know" is the expected path, not a coercion failure.
+    expect(warnSpy).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null and logs a Warn for confident:true with a missing priceUsd', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: true });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null and logs a Warn for confident:true with a non-numeric priceUsd', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: true, priceUsd: '189.50' });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null and logs a Warn for confident:true with a zero priceUsd', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: true, priceUsd: 0 });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null and logs a Warn for confident:true with a negative priceUsd', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: true, priceUsd: -45 });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null and logs a Warn for confident:true with a non-finite priceUsd (JSON numeric overflow to Infinity)', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+    // 1e400 is valid JSON syntax but overflows a double to Infinity on parse —
+    // unlike the bare token `Infinity`, which is not valid JSON at all and
+    // would be caught by the not-valid-JSON branch instead, never reaching
+    // the Number.isFinite guard this test means to exercise.
+    const raw = '{"confident":true,"priceUsd":1e400}';
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null without throwing for a plain-English refusal (non-JSON prose)', () => {
+    const warnSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'warn').mockImplementation(() => undefined);
+
+    expect(() => parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse("I don't have enough data to estimate that.")).not.toThrow();
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse("I don't have enough data to estimate that.")).toBeNull();
+    expect(warnSpy).toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null for a price range instead of a single number', () => {
+    const raw = JSON.stringify({ confident: true, priceUsd: '150-200' });
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null for "no price found" style text', () => {
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse('No price found for this part.')).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('returns null for null as the parsed JSON, without throwing', () => {
+    expect(() => parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse('null')).not.toThrow();
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse('null')).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('strips a markdown code fence the model added despite being told not to', () => {
+    const raw = '```json\n{"confident":true,"priceUsd":42}\n```';
+
+    expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBe(42);
+  });
+
+  it('never invents a number: every non-clean-confident-numeric shape coerces to null', () => {
+    // Malformed in every conceivable way
+    const testCases = [
+      { confident: true, priceUsd: null },
+      { confident: true, priceUsd: undefined },
+      { confident: true, priceUsd: {} },
+      { confident: true, priceUsd: [] },
+      { confident: true, priceUsd: 'free' },
+      { confident: 'yes', priceUsd: 25 },
+      { priceUsd: 25 },
+      {},
+    ];
+
+    for (const testCase of testCases) {
+      const raw = JSON.stringify(testCase);
+      expect(parser.parsePriceEstimationResponse(raw)).toBeNull();
+    }
+  });
+});

 <section class="panel">
 <h1>Feature</h1>
-<p class="note">Status done &middot; cycle 1 of 3</p>
-<h2>Goal</h2><p>Right now, `POST /api/listings/:id/identify-parts` gives exactly one brand and one model/part number per part, or `null` if it can&#x27;t name one confidently. This feature adds a layer under that: when the AI can&#x27;t pin a part down to one answer, but can narrow it to a short list of likely answers, it reports that list instead of just giving up with `null`. That means multiple possible brands when the brand itself is unclear, or multiple possible models within one known brand when only the model is unclear. The list is saved with the Expected Part (not just shown once and thrown away), carries over to the Part made at teardown the same way brand/model already do, and shows up everywhere a part&#x27;s identity already shows up. Pricing these candidates is a separate, later feature and out of scope here — this feature only makes and keeps the list.</p>
-<h2>Requirements</h2><pre>1. If the AI can confidently name one brand and one model for a part, nothing changes: `brand`/`partNumber` stay single values and no candidate list is made.
-2. If the AI has no lead at all on a part&#x27;s brand or model, nothing changes: the field stays `null` and no candidate list is made for it — an empty list isn&#x27;t a guess.
-3. If the AI can narrow a part to a short list of likely brands but can&#x27;t pick one, it reports that list as ranked candidates (most to least likely), each paired with a model/part number where one is known for that candidate — instead of leaving `brand` as `null` with nothing else.
-4. If the AI confidently knows a part&#x27;s brand but can only narrow its model/part number to a short list, it reports that brand along with the list of likely models as candidates — instead of leaving `partNumber` as `null` with nothing else.
-5. A candidate list is capped at a small fixed size, never has two identical {brand, model} pairs, and never repeats the single confident answer already reported for that field.
-6. Once the user saves the Listing, an Expected Part&#x27;s candidate list is saved with it — not dropped at save time, even if the user never picks one of the candidates.
-7. When a Purchase is torn down, an Expected Part&#x27;s saved candidate list carries over to the Part made for it, the same way its brand/partNumber already carry over.
-8. Everywhere an Expected Part&#x27;s or Part&#x27;s identified brand/model already shows up today — the Listing&#x27;s Expected Parts editor, the Purchase detail screen&#x27;s Expected Parts table, the Teardown checklist, the Parts list table, and the Record Sale page heading — a non-empty candidate list shows next to the main identification, not just stored out of sight.
-9. In the Listing&#x27;s Expected Parts editor, picking one of a row&#x27;s candidates fills that row&#x27;s brand/model fields in one action; picking one is never required before the row can be saved.</pre>
-<p><a href=".claude/feature/ai-identification-ranges/prototype.html (local file — no hosted-publish capability on this host, so there is no live URL)">Prototype</a></p>

-- **Cycle 1 existed because of one recurring failure mode, not a new one.** T5&#x27;s `listing-form.tsx` change correctly added `identityCandidates: []` to every row of the Listing POST/PUT payload (Req 6). A pre-existing exact-shape `toEqual()` assertion in `apps/web/test/identify-parts.test.tsx` — a file outside T5&#x27;s and TS5&#x27;s own touched-file list — broke as a result. Per the one-free-retry-per-task-per-cycle rule, this second distinct T5 failure opened Cycle 1 as task T7 (a 3-line test-only fix), plus its review pair R7/TS7.  ## Proposals
 
-- **When a plan task adds a required field to a type/interface that existing code already constructs (`Create*Input`, a row/props type, a parsed-output shape), its own task description should explicitly name every pre-existing test file that constructs or asserts on that shape, not just the files where new logic lives.** This feature&#x27;s plan already named each task&#x27;s *production* files carefully; it&#x27;s the *existing test fixtures the new field will break* that went unnamed five times (T2, T3, T5×2, T6) and once underspecified in the other direction (T4&#x27;s validation/carry-over tests). A one-line scope addition like &quot;also update `db.test.ts`&#x27;s `createExpectedPart`/`createPart` call sites and the two column-order assertions to include the new field&quot; would have let T2&#x27;s own builder fix it in the first pass instead of costing a full must_fix/retry round.
+- **When a plan task adds a required field to a type/interface that existing code already constructs (<code>Create*Input</code>, a row/props type, a parsed-output shape), its own task description should explicitly name every pre-existing test file that constructs or asserts on that shape, not just the files where new logic lives.** This feature&#x27;s plan already named each task&#x27;s *production* files carefully; it&#x27;s the *existing test fixtures the new field will break* that went unnamed five times (T2, T3, T5×2, T6) and once underspecified in the other direction (T4&#x27;s validation/carry-over tests). A one-line scope addition like &quot;also update <code>db.test.ts</code>&#x27;s <code>createExpectedPart</code>/<code>createPart</code> call sites and the two column-order assertions to include the new field&quot; would have let T2&#x27;s own builder fix it in the first pass instead of costing a full must_fix/retry round.
 
-- **Prefer `identityCandidates?: X[]` (optional, default `[]` at the read site) over a required field for any shape with pre-existing callers**, the same way T4&#x27;s validator already defaults an absent field to `[]`. T2 made `CreateExpectedPartInput`/`CreatePartInput` required specifically to match sibling-field convention (A8), and that choice is what turned every untouched caller into a runtime throw instead of a silent, correct default. The convention-consistency argument is real, but for a cross-cutting additive field it cost more in review/retry rounds than it bought in type-strictness; a future feature with this exact shape (new field on an existing, widely-constructed type) should default to optional+fallback and only go required if a specific caller needs the compiler to force it.
+- **Prefer <code>identityCandidates?: X[]</code> (optional, default <code>[]</code> at the read site) over a required field for any shape with pre-existing callers**, the same way T4&#x27;s validator already defaults an absent field to <code>[]</code>. T2 made <code>CreateExpectedPartInput</code>/<code>CreatePartInput</code> required specifically to match sibling-field convention (A8), and that choice is what turned every untouched caller into a runtime throw instead of a silent, correct default. The convention-consistency argument is real, but for a cross-cutting additive field it cost more in review/retry rounds than it bought in type-strictness; a future feature with this exact shape (new field on an existing, widely-constructed type) should default to optional+fallback and only go required if a specific caller needs the compiler to force it.
 
-- **Run a full-suite standing gate once, immediately after the first wave of builders lands, before dispatching any reviewers/testers**, rather than discovering the breadth of the fixture-break pattern piecemeal across five separate review findings. feature-validate-lead&#x27;s cycle-0 gate did catch all of T2/T3/T5/T6&#x27;s breaks in one pass (good), but R4/R6 still had to independently rediscover variants of the same root cause in their own task-scoped reviews. A single upfront `npm run test` pass surfaced to the orchestrator before task-scoped review dispatch would have let every reviewer start from &quot;here are the N pre-existing tests this task&#x27;s required field broke&quot; instead of each reviewer finding their own slice.

-<h2>Assumptions</h2><ul><li>A1 (cycle 0): `MAX_IDENTITY_CANDIDATES = 4` — a reasonable small cap balancing usefulness against UI clutter; not specified by the request. Easy to tune later; it&#x27;s one named constant, not scattered magic numbers.</li><li>A2 (cycle 0): Picking an alternate candidate in the editor keeps `aiSuggested: true` — it&#x27;s still an AI-origin value, just a different one of the AI&#x27;s own candidates. +// Polls `output()` until it matches `pattern` or `timeoutMs` elapses. Used
+// below to wait for a background service's own startup log line, which can
+// land a tick or two after /health first responds 200 (e.g.
+// pricePartsWorkerPool.ts's startup line is logged only once its orphan-
+// reset query resolves, not synchronously on call). Real setTimeout, not
+// fake timers, by necessity: what's being awaited is a real child
+// process's own stdout write landing via real OS pipe I/O on the real
+// platform clock (same rationale as waitForHealth above, and the whole
+// suite's real-OS-signal pattern) — this process's fake timers have no
+// effect on when the subprocess itself logs.
+async function waitForOutputPattern(pattern: RegExp, timeoutMs: number, output: () => string): Promise<void> {
+  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
+  while (Date.now() < deadline) {
+    if (pattern.test(output())) {
+      return;
+    }
+    const { promise: waited, resolve: resolveWait } = Promise.withResolvers<void>();
+    setTimeout(resolveWait, 150);
+    await waited;
+  }
+  throw new Error(`Output never matched ${pattern} within ${timeoutMs}ms\n--- server output ---\n${output()}`);
+}

+// Proof for the part-price-estimation feature's pure-wiring task: index.ts
+// must call startPriceRefreshScheduler() and startPricePartsWorkerPool()
+// at boot (mirroring the pre-existing scan scheduler/identify-parts pool
+// immediately above) and must include their returned handles' .stop()
+// calls in gracefulShutdown() alongside the two pre-existing ones. Reuses
+// this file's own spawnServer/waitForHealth real-child-process pattern
+// rather than mocking index.ts, which has no exported build function (see
+// this file's header comment).
+//
+// What this suite can and cannot prove, by construction: "registered at
+// boot" is directly provable — each service logs its own distinct startup
+// line (see waitForOutputPattern calls below) only when index.ts actually
+// calls its start function, so removing either `const ... = startX()` line
+// makes the corresponding assertion fail. "Stopped on SIGTERM" is NOT
+// further provable by this (or any) black-box process-exit observation:
+// gracefulShutdown() in index.ts ends with an unconditional process.exit(0)
+// (see index.ts), which force-terminates the process immediately
+// regardless of any interval/cron timer still registered — so a dangling,
+// never-.stop()'d timer produces an identical clean, non-hung exit to one
+// that was properly stopped; there is no observable difference to assert
+// on from outside the process. What a missing `.stop()` call on a handle
+// that doesn't exist or has the wrong shape WOULD change is whether
+// gracefulShutdown()'s synchronous `x.stop()` call throws, which flips the
+// outcome from "Graceful shutdown complete" to "Graceful shutdown failed"
+// (handleShutdownFailure) and exit code 1 instead of 0 — the assertions
+// below do check for exactly that failure text, so a handle shape bug
+// (e.g. `stop` missing/renamed) is caught, even though a
+// present-but-silently-omitted `.stop()` call is not. This exact
+// limitation already applies identically to the two pre-existing handles
+// (scheduledTask, identifyPartsPool): none of the three test cases above
+// in this same file assert on clearInterval, exit code, or hung-handle
+// detection for either of them — only WAL-flush artifacts and a
+// `/graceful shutdown/i` substring match that (confirmed by inspection)
+// matches both the "complete" and "failed" log lines equally, since both
+// contain the substring "graceful shutdown". So this suite's coverage of
+// the two NEW handles is deliberately brought up to exact parity with the
+// two pre-existing ones, not held to a stronger, unachievable standard.
+describe('price research background services (part-price-estimation feature): started at boot, wired into graceful shutdown', () => {
+  // Own data dir/port, independent of the three suites above, so this
+  // suite's single spawn+SIGTERM cycle never races their sequential/
+  // double-signal/SIGHUP-race cycles.
+  const wiringPort = TEST_PORT + 3;
+  let wiringDataDir: string;
+
+  beforeAll(async () => {
+    wiringDataDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'pc-parts-flipper-shutdown-price-wiring-'));
+  });
+
+  afterAll(async () => {
+    await rm(wiringDataDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  });
+
+  it(
+    'registers the price-refresh scheduler and price-parts worker pool at boot, and the SIGTERM shutdown sequence (including their .stop() calls) completes without error',
+    async () => {
+      const instance = await spawnServer(wiringDataDir, wiringPort);
+
+      // Registered at boot: each new service logs its own distinct startup
+      // line (see priceRefreshScheduler.ts/pricePartsWorkerPool.ts) the
+      // moment index.ts's top-level startPriceRefreshScheduler()/
+      // startPricePartsWorkerPool() calls run — distinct strings from the
+      // pre-existing scan scheduler's "Registering scheduled scan job" and
+      // identify-parts pool's "Recovered orphaned identify-parts jobs", so
+      // these can only be satisfied by the two NEW services actually
+      // starting, not their pre-existing counterparts.
+      await waitForOutputPattern(/Registering scheduled price refresh job/, 10_000, () => instance.output());
+      await waitForOutputPattern(/Recovered orphaned price-parts jobs/, 10_000, () => instance.output());
+
+      instance.sendSignal('SIGTERM');
+      await instance.waitForExit();
+
+      // The shutdown sequence ran gracefulShutdown()'s full body — which
+      // now calls .stop() on both new handles alongside the two
+      // pre-existing ones — and reached its final success log line rather
+      // than throwing partway through and falling into
+      // handleShutdownFailure's "Graceful shutdown failed" branch. Checked
+      // as an exact phrase (not the earlier tests' looser /graceful
+      // shutdown/i, which matches both outcomes) so this assertion alone
+      // distinguishes success from failure.
+      expect(instance.output()).toMatch(/Graceful shutdown complete/);
+      expect(instance.output()).not.toMatch(/Graceful shutdown failed/);
+    },
+    30_000,
+  );
+});

    +// T4 proof: the identify-parts write path claims every fully-identified
+// part it persists for the price pipeline and hands the saved rows to the
+// trigger (part-price-estimation feature, Req 1 and 3).
+//
+// Isolated the same way identifyPartsWorkerPool.test.ts isolates the pool —
+// the db/index.js barrel and ai/partIdentification.js are mocked — plus
+// jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js, so what is asserted here is the wiring
+// (priceSource decided per part, trigger called with the created rows) and
+// not the trigger's own decision, which pricePartsTrigger.test.ts proves
+// separately.
+import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
+import type { IdentifyPartsJob, IdentifyPartsResponse } from '@pc-parts-flipper/shared';
+import type { CreateExpectedPartInput, ExpectedPart, Listing } from '../src/db/index.js';
+import { logger } from '../src/logger.js';
+
+const claimNextQueuedJob = vi.fn<[], Promise<IdentifyPartsJob | null>>();
+const completeJob = vi.fn<[string, IdentifyPartsResponse], Promise<void>>();
+const failJob = vi.fn<[string, string], Promise<void>>();
+const resetOrphanedJobsToQueued = vi.fn<[], Promise<string[]>>();
+const updateJobStage = vi.fn<[string, 'sourcing_references' | 'comparing'], Promise<void>>();
+const getListingById = vi.fn<[string], Promise<Listing | null>>();
+const createExpectedPart = vi.fn<[CreateExpectedPartInput], Promise<ExpectedPart>>();
+
+vi.mock('../src/db/index.js', () => ({
+  claimNextQueuedJob: (...args: []) => claimNextQueuedJob(...args),
+  completeJob: (...args: [string, IdentifyPartsResponse]) => completeJob(...args),
+  failJob: (...args: [string, string]) => failJob(...args),
+  resetOrphanedJobsToQueued: (...args: []) => resetOrphanedJobsToQueued(...args),
+  updateJobStage: (...args: [string, 'sourcing_references' | 'comparing']) => updateJobStage(...args),
+  getListingById: (...args: [string]) => getListingById(...args),
+  createExpectedPart: (...args: [CreateExpectedPartInput]) => createExpectedPart(...args),
+}));
+
+const triggerPriceLookups = vi.fn<[ExpectedPart[]], Promise<void>>();
+vi.mock('../src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js', () => ({
+  triggerPriceLookups: (...args: [ExpectedPart[]]) => triggerPriceLookups(...args),
+}));
+
+import { startIdentifyPartsWorkerPool } from '../src/jobs/identifyPartsWorkerPool.js';
+
+function listingFixture(id: string): Listing {
+  return {
+    id,
+    sourceId: 'source-1',
+    title: 'Test listing',
+    description: null,
+    listingUrl: null,
+    askingPrice: 100,
+    listingDate: '2026-01-01',
+    status: 'open',
+    acquisitionFees: 0,
+    photoPaths: [],
+    externalSource: null,
+    externalItemId: null,
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+  };
+}
+
+function jobFixture(id: string, listingId: string): IdentifyPartsJob {
+  return {
+    id,
+    listingId,
+    stage: 'identifying',
+    parts: null,
+    notes: null,
+    errorMessage: null,
+    delivered: false,
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    startedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    completedAt: null,
+  };
+}
+
+// Echoes the insert back as the stored row would read, so the test can
+// assert on exactly what the trigger was handed.
+function storedRow(input: CreateExpectedPartInput, id: string): ExpectedPart {
+  return {
+    id,
+    listingId: input.listingId,
+    componentCategory: input.componentCategory,
+    brand: input.brand,
+    partNumber: input.partNumber,
+    description: input.description,
+    estimatedResaleValue: input.estimatedResaleValue,
+    identityCandidates: input.identityCandidates,
+    aiConfidence: input.aiConfidence,
+    aiSource: input.aiSource,
+    priceSource: input.priceSource ?? null,
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+  };
+}
+
+type OnStage = (stage: 'sourcing_references' | 'comparing') => void;
+const identifyListingParts = vi.fn<[Listing, unknown, OnStage | undefined], Promise<IdentifyPartsResponse>>();
+vi.mock('../src/ai/partIdentification.js', () => ({
+  identifyListingParts: (...args: [Listing, unknown, OnStage | undefined]) => identifyListingParts(...args),
+}));
+
+let activeHandle: { stop(): void } | null = null;
+
+beforeEach(() => {
+  vi.clearAllMocks();
+  claimNextQueuedJob.mockResolvedValue(null);
+  resetOrphanedJobsToQueued.mockResolvedValue([]);
+  getListingById.mockResolvedValue(null);
+  triggerPriceLookups.mockResolvedValue(undefined);
+  let rowCounter = 0;
+  createExpectedPart.mockImplementation(async (input) => {
+    rowCounter += 1;
+    return storedRow(input, `row-${rowCounter}`);
+  });
+  vi.spyOn(logger, 'info').mockImplementation(() => logger);
+  vi.spyOn(logger, 'error').mockImplementation(() => logger);
+});
+
+afterEach(() => {
+  activeHandle?.stop();
+  activeHandle = null;
+  vi.restoreAllMocks();
+});

+describe('persistIdentifiedParts — price pipeline hand-off (Req 1, 3)', () => {
+  it('claims a fully-identified part, leaves a half-identified one unclaimed, and triggers once with every saved row', async () => {
+    const listing = listingFixture('listing-P');
+    claimNextQueuedJob.mockResolvedValueOnce(jobFixture('job-P', 'listing-P')).mockResolvedValue(null);
+    getListingById.mockResolvedValue(listing);
+    identifyListingParts.mockResolvedValue({
+      parts: [
+        { category: 'GPU', brand: 'NVIDIA', partNumber: 'RTX 3060', description: 'Card', confidence: 'high', source: 'image', identityCandidates: [] },
+        { category: 'Storage', brand: 'Samsung', partNumber: null, description: 'NVMe', confidence: 'low', source: 'text', identityCandidates: [] },
+        { category: 'RAM', brand: null, partNumber: null, description: '16GB', confidence: 'low', source: 'text', identityCandidates: [] },
+      ],
+      notes: 'done',
+    });
+
+    activeHandle = startIdentifyPartsWorkerPool();
+
+    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(completeJob).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1));
+
+    const inserts = createExpectedPart.mock.calls.map(([input]) => input);
+    expect(inserts).toHaveLength(3);
+    // Req 1: brand + part number both known -> the pipeline owns the price.
+    expect(inserts.find((i) => i.componentCategory === 'GPU')?.priceSource).toBe('auto');
+    // Req 3: a part missing either half is never auto-researched, so it is
+    // never claimed either.
+    expect(inserts.find((i) => i.componentCategory === 'Storage')?.priceSource).toBeNull();
+    expect(inserts.find((i) => i.componentCategory === 'RAM')?.priceSource).toBeNull();
+
+    // One hand-off, after every row is in, carrying the stored rows (not the
+    // model's raw output) — the trigger reads each row's own fields.
+    expect(triggerPriceLookups).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    const handedOver = triggerPriceLookups.mock.calls[0][0];
+    expect(handedOver.map((part) => part.componentCategory).sort()).toEqual(['GPU', 'RAM', 'Storage']);
+    expect(handedOver.find((part) => part.componentCategory === 'GPU')?.priceSource).toBe('auto');
+  });
+
+  // The job must not report success over a hand-off that never happened:
+  // persistIdentifiedParts awaits the trigger, so a throwing trigger fails
+  // the job the same way a failing insert already does.
+  it('fails the job rather than completing it when the trigger throws', async () => {
+    const listing = listingFixture('listing-Q');
+    claimNextQueuedJob.mockResolvedValueOnce(jobFixture('job-Q', 'listing-Q')).mockResolvedValue(null);
+    getListingById.mockResolvedValue(listing);
+    identifyListingParts.mockResolvedValue({
+      parts: [
+        { category: 'CPU', brand: 'Intel', partNumber: 'i7-9700K', description: 'Chip', confidence: 'high', source: 'text', identityCandidates: [] },
+      ],
+      notes: '',
+    });
+    triggerPriceLookups.mockRejectedValue(new Error('price trigger exploded'));
+
+    activeHandle = startIdentifyPartsWorkerPool();
+
+    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(failJob).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1));
+    expect(failJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('job-Q', 'price trigger exploded');
+    expect(completeJob).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+});
