    +// Tests for apps/server/src/db/queries/partPriceCache.ts
+// (part-price-estimation feature T3).
+import type * as DbModule from '../src/db/index.js';
+import type * as ClientModule from '../src/db/client.js';
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
+import { runMigrations } from '../src/db/migrate.js';
+
+let dataDir: string;
+let db: typeof DbModule;
+let getConnection: typeof ClientModule.getConnection;
+
+beforeAll(async () => {
+  dataDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'pc-parts-flipper-price-cache-test-'));
+  process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR = dataDir;
+  // Exception (ts-no-dynamic-import): client.ts's DATA_DIR is computed once,
+  // from DUCKDB_DATA_DIR, at module-evaluation time into a file-scoped const.
+  // A static top-of-file import would evaluate before this beforeAll sets the
+  // env var and would therefore open the shared dev database instead of this
+  // test's isolated temp dir — the dynamic import here is what makes the
+  // module-loading timing land after the env var.
+  db = await import('../src/db/index.js') as typeof DbModule;
+  ({ getConnection } = await import('../src/db/client.js') as typeof ClientModule);
+  await runMigrations(await getConnection());
+});
+
+afterAll(async () => {
+  delete process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR;
+  await rm(dataDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+});
+
+// Direct row count for one (category, brand, part_number) combo, bypassing
+// the query module under test so the assertion doesn't depend on the same
+// code it's verifying.
+async function countCacheRows(category: string, brand: string, partNumber: string): Promise<number> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT COUNT(*) as cnt FROM part_price_cache
+     WHERE component_category = $category AND brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number`,
+    { category, brand, part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return Number(reader.getRowObjectsJson()[0].cnt);
+}
+
+// Seeds one expected_parts row (via a Source/Listing) with the given brand/
+// partNumber/price_source, for propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts tests.
+async function seedExpectedPart(
+  category: 'GPU' | 'CPU' | 'RAM',
+  brand: string,
+  partNumber: string,
+  priceSource: 'auto' | null,
+  estimatedResaleValue: number | null,
+): Promise<string> {
+  const source = await db.createSource({
+    name: `Test Source ${Math.random()}`,
+    platformType: 'other',
+    url: null,
+    notes: null,
+  });
+  const listing = await db.createListing({
+    sourceId: source.id,
+    title: 'Test Listing',
+    description: null,
+    listingUrl: null,
+    askingPrice: 100,
+    listingDate: new Date().toISOString(),
+    acquisitionFees: 0,
+    photoPaths: [],
+    externalSource: null,
+    externalItemId: null,
+  });
+  const expectedPart = await db.createExpectedPart({
+    listingId: listing.id,
+    componentCategory: category,
+    brand,
+    partNumber,
+    description: 'Test part',
+    estimatedResaleValue,
+    identityCandidates: [],
+    aiConfidence: null,
+    aiSource: null,
+  });
+  if (priceSource !== null) {
+    const connection = await getConnection();
+    await connection.run(
+      `UPDATE expected_parts SET price_source = $price_source WHERE id = $id`,
+      { id: expectedPart.id, price_source: priceSource },
+    );
+  }
+  return expectedPart.id;
+}
+
+describe('Part Price Cache — findPriceCacheEntry', () => {
+  it('returns null before any insertPriceCacheEntry and the row after', async () => {
+    const before = await db.findPriceCacheEntry('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 4070');
+    expect(before).toBeNull();
+
+    await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 4070');
+
+    const after = await db.findPriceCacheEntry('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 4070');
+    expect(after).not.toBeNull();
+    expect(after?.category).toBe('GPU');
+    expect(after?.brand).toBe('NVIDIA');
+    expect(after?.partNumber).toBe('RTX 4070');
+    expect(after?.estimatedResaleValue).toBeNull();
+    expect(after?.lastResearchedAt).toBeNull();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('Part Price Cache — insertPriceCacheEntry', () => {
+  it('inserts a new row with null estimated_resale_value and null last_researched_at', async () => {
+    const { inserted } = await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('CPU', 'Intel', 'Core i9-13900K');
+    expect(inserted).toBe(true);
+
+    const connection = await getConnection();
+    const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+      `SELECT estimated_resale_value, last_researched_at FROM part_price_cache
+       WHERE component_category = 'CPU' AND brand = 'Intel' AND part_number = 'Core i9-13900K'`,
+    );
+    const rows = reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(rows[0].estimated_resale_value).toBeNull();
+    expect(rows[0].last_researched_at).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  // Dedup proof: a second insert for the identical (category, brand,
+  // part_number) combo must be silently skipped (no throw, { inserted:
+  // false }), and the unique index must be the thing actually preventing
+  // the second row — not just the query module choosing not to insert. If
+  // idx_part_price_cache_dedup were dropped from schema.ts's
+  // INDEX_STATEMENTS, this test would fail: both inserts would succeed and
+  // the row count would be 2, not 1.
+  it('returns { inserted: false } and skips a second insert for the identical combo (unique index dedup)', async () => {
+    const first = await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('RAM', 'Corsair', 'Vengeance LPX 32GB');
+    expect(first.inserted).toBe(true);
+
+    const second = await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('RAM', 'Corsair', 'Vengeance LPX 32GB');
+    expect(second.inserted).toBe(false);
+
+    const count = await countCacheRows('RAM', 'Corsair', 'Vengeance LPX 32GB');
+    expect(count).toBe(1);
+  });
+
+  // Concurrency proof: two GENUINELY concurrent inserts for the identical
+  // combo (fired together via Promise.all, not sequential awaits) must
+  // never produce two rows or an unhandled rejection — same proof style as
+  // referencePhotos.test.ts / sourcingAttempts.test.ts.
+  it('dedups two genuinely concurrent insertPriceCacheEntry calls for the identical combo (real race)', async () => {
+    const results = await Promise.all([
+      db.insertPriceCacheEntry('GPU', 'AMD', 'RX 7900 XTX'),
+      db.insertPriceCacheEntry('GPU', 'AMD', 'RX 7900 XTX'),
+    ]);
+
+    const count = await countCacheRows('GPU', 'AMD', 'RX 7900 XTX');
+    expect(count).toBe(1);
+    expect(results.filter((r) => r.inserted).length).toBe(1);
+  });
+
+  it('does not swallow a non-dedup DB error', async () => {
+    // An invalid component_category (not one of the enum's fixed values)
+    // triggers an ENUM-cast error, not a unique-index violation. The catch
+    // block in insertPriceCacheEntry only recognizes and swallows the
+    // unique-index "already cached, skip" case (UNIQUE_CONSTRAINT_MESSAGE);
+    // every other DB error must propagate to the caller unchanged.
+    await expect(
+      db.insertPriceCacheEntry('NotARealCategory' as unknown as 'GPU', 'Bogus', 'Bogus'),
+    ).rejects.toThrow();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('Part Price Cache — updatePriceCacheEntryValue', () => {
+  it('sets estimated_resale_value and last_researched_at when value is a number', async () => {
+    await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('Storage', 'Samsung', '990 Pro 2TB');
+
+    await db.updatePriceCacheEntryValue('Storage', 'Samsung', '990 Pro 2TB', 150.5);
+
+    const entry = await db.findPriceCacheEntry('Storage', 'Samsung', '990 Pro 2TB');
+    expect(entry?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(150.5);
+    expect(entry?.lastResearchedAt).not.toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('sets only last_researched_at when value is null, leaving estimated_resale_value untouched', async () => {
+    await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('PSU', 'Seasonic', 'Focus GX-850');
+
+    await db.updatePriceCacheEntryValue('PSU', 'Seasonic', 'Focus GX-850', null);
+
+    const entry = await db.findPriceCacheEntry('PSU', 'Seasonic', 'Focus GX-850');
+    expect(entry?.estimatedResaleValue).toBeNull();
+    expect(entry?.lastResearchedAt).not.toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  // Standing Checks guarantee: once a combo has a real priced value, a
+  // later null-finding refresh (research ran again but couldn't price it
+  // this time) must never regress estimated_resale_value back to null.
+  // Proven both by direct assertion here AND by the mutation runner below,
+  // which confirms this exact test fails if the production code's null
+  // branch is changed to also null out estimated_resale_value.
+  it('never nulls out a previously-set estimated_resale_value on a later null-value update (regression guard)', async () => {
+    await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('Case', 'Fractal Design', 'Meshify 2');
+    await db.updatePriceCacheEntryValue('Case', 'Fractal Design', 'Meshify 2', 75);
+
+    const afterPriced = await db.findPriceCacheEntry('Case', 'Fractal Design', 'Meshify 2');
+    expect(afterPriced?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(75);
+
+    // A later refresh finds no price this time.
+    await db.updatePriceCacheEntryValue('Case', 'Fractal Design', 'Meshify 2', null);
+
+    const afterNullRefresh = await db.findPriceCacheEntry('Case', 'Fractal Design', 'Meshify 2');
+    expect(afterNullRefresh?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(75);
+  });
+});
+
+describe('Part Price Cache — listAllPriceCacheEntries', () => {
+  it('returns every cached entry, including ones never successfully priced', async () => {
+    await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('Cooler', 'Noctua', 'NH-D15');
+    await db.insertPriceCacheEntry('Cooler', 'Corsair', 'H150i');
+    await db.updatePriceCacheEntryValue('Cooler', 'Noctua', 'NH-D15', 40);
+
+    const all = await db.listAllPriceCacheEntries();
+    const noctua = all.find((e) => e.brand === 'Noctua' && e.partNumber === 'NH-D15');
+    const corsair = all.find((e) => e.brand === 'Corsair' && e.partNumber === 'H150i');
+    expect(noctua?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(40);
+    expect(corsair?.estimatedResaleValue).toBeNull();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('Part Price Cache — propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts', () => {
+  it('updates an expected_parts row whose price_source is auto', async () => {
+    const id = await seedExpectedPart('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3080', 'auto', 50);
+
+    await db.propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3080', 300);
+
+    const updated = await db.getExpectedPartById(id);
+    expect(updated?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(300);
+  });
+
+  // Load-bearing guard: a manually-claimed row (price_source null) must
+  // never be overwritten by a background cache propagation, even when its
+  // category/brand/partNumber combo matches exactly.
+  it('does not touch an expected_parts row whose price_source is null (manually entered/claimed)', async () => {
+    const id = await seedExpectedPart('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3080 Ti', null, 999);
+
+    await db.propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3080 Ti', 1);
+
+    const untouched = await db.getExpectedPartById(id);
+    expect(untouched?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(999);
+  });
+});
  • @@ -0,0 +1,456 @@
    +// T4 standing-check proof, HTTP half: the price pipeline's triggering
+// behaviour through the REAL Listing write paths, against a REAL server and
+// a REAL isolated DuckDB file (helpers.ts's spawn-based fixture, the same
+// one concurrency.test.ts and identifyPartsConcurrency.test.ts use).
+//
+// What this suite adds over priceSourceRoute.test.ts — which proves the
+// `price_source` column threads through those same routes — is the pipeline
+// state each save leaves behind in part_price_cache and price_parts_jobs,
+// and what happens when two saves land on the same combination at once:
+//
+//   * Req 13: two (and then a PUT plus a POST) genuinely racing first
+//     encounters of the same (component_category, brand, part_number) must
+//     leave exactly one cache row and exactly one non-terminal job. Fired
+//     through Promise.all — both requests are in flight before either has
+//     read the cache, which is the only state that can produce the
+//     duplicate at all. Two sequential saves would prove nothing here.
+//   * Req 4 + Req 5: a save enqueues research rather than performing it —
+//     the request returns 201/200 while the job it left behind is still
+//     `queued` and the cache row it created is still unpriced.
+//   * Req 3: a part missing brand or part number leaves no cache row and no
+//     job at all, not merely a null `price_source`.
+//   * Req 6 + Req 7 + Req 14: a combo already carrying a cached value (seeded
+//     into the database before the server boots, so it demonstrably belongs
+//     to no Listing in this suite) is applied to a new Listing's auto row
+//     immediately with no job enqueued, while a row the user priced himself
+//     in that same request keeps his figure.
+//   * Req 14 across a resave: resaving an auto row that is still waiting for
+//     its price neither duplicates the job nor disturbs the cache row.
+//
+// Every HTTP call runs in beforeAll and the resulting rows are read
+// afterwards, in one pass, because DuckDB holds an exclusive lock on the
+// database file while the server process owns it — a second connection
+// cannot be opened alongside the running server (see
+// priceSourceRoute.test.ts's own note on the same constraint). The `it`
+// blocks below are therefore assertions over that captured state.
+//
+// IDENTIFY_PARTS_POOL_SIZE=0 and PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE=0 keep both worker
+// pools from claiming anything, so the real `omp` binary is never invoked
+// (this project's standing rule that the suite stays network- and
+// credential-independent) and every job this suite enqueues stays exactly
+// as the write path left it.
+import type { DuckDBConnection } from '@duckdb/node-api';
+import type { CreateListingInput, ListingDetail } from '@pc-parts-flipper/shared';
+import type * as ClientModule from '../src/db/client.js';
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
+import { runMigrations } from '../src/db/migrate.js';
+import { getJson, postJson, startTestServer, type JsonResponse, type TestServer } from './helpers.js';
+
+// Arbitrary high port distinct from every other suite's TEST_PORT (highest
+// currently in use is priceSourceRoute.test.ts's 34_640).
+const TEST_PORT = 34_641;
+
+interface SourceResponse {
+  id: string;
+}
+
+type PartInput = CreateListingInput['expectedParts'][number];
+
+// PUTs `body` as JSON to `url`. helpers.ts has no PUT wrapper (see
+// listings.test.ts and priceSourceRoute.test.ts's identical local copies).
+async function putJson<T>(url: string, body: unknown): Promise<JsonResponse<T>> {
+  const res = await fetch(url, { method: 'PUT', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify(body) });
+  return { status: res.status, body: (await res.json()) as T };
+}
+
+/** One Expected Part request row; every caller below overrides only what its scenario is about. */
+function part(overrides: Partial<PartInput> & Pick<PartInput, 'componentCategory'>): PartInput {
+  return {
+    brand: null,
+    partNumber: null,
+    description: 'Pulled from the tower',
+    estimatedResaleValue: null,
+    identityCandidates: [],
+    aiConfidence: null,
+    aiSource: null,
+    ...overrides,
+  } as PartInput;
+}
+
+function listingBody(sourceId: string, title: string, expectedParts: PartInput[]): CreateListingInput {
+  return {
+    sourceId,
+    title,
+    description: null,
+    listingUrl: 'https://example.com/listing/price-concurrency',
+    askingPrice: 200,
+    listingDate: '2026-03-01',
+    acquisitionFees: 0,
+    expectedParts,
+  };
+}
+
+// The combos each scenario owns. Distinct part numbers mean the row counts
+// below can only be explained by the scenario that produced them.
+const RACE_TWO_SAVES = 'HTTP-RACE-A';
+const RACE_PUT_VS_POST = 'HTTP-RACE-B';
+const PUT_ONLY = 'HTTP-PUT-ONLY';
+const RESAVE_AUTO = 'HTTP-RESAVE-D';
+const USER_PRICED = 'HTTP-USEREDIT-E';
+const HALF_IDENTIFIED = 'HTTP-HALF-F';
+const PRE_CACHED = 'HTTP-CACHED-G';
+// Seeded before boot, so the only way a Listing in this suite can show it is
+// the cache. Written as a literal here and asserted as a literal below.
+const PRE_CACHED_VALUE = 250;
+// What the user types on his own row of that same pre-cached combo.
+const USER_TYPED_VALUE = 95;
+
+let dataDir: string;
+let server: TestServer;
+let serverStopped = false;
+
+// Captured HTTP results.
+let raceTwoSaves: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>[];
+let racePutVsPost: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>[];
+let editOnlySaved: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+let resaveCreated: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+let resaveUpdated: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+let userPricedCreated: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+let userPricedUpdated: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+let halfIdentified: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+let cacheHitListing: JsonResponse<ListingDetail>;
+
+let connection: DuckDBConnection;
+
+/** Raw cache rows for one part number, read without going through the query module under test. */
+async function cacheRows(partNumber: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>[]> {
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT component_category, brand, estimated_resale_value, last_researched_at
+     FROM part_price_cache WHERE part_number = $part_number`,
+    { part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+}
+
+/** Raw job rows for one part number, likewise read directly. */
+async function jobRows(partNumber: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>[]> {
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT id, stage FROM price_parts_jobs WHERE part_number = $part_number`,
+    { part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+}
+
+beforeAll(async () => {
+  dataDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'pc-parts-flipper-price-concurrency-'));
+  process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR = dataDir;
+  // Exception (ts-no-dynamic-import), same reason identifyPartsJobsRoute.
+  // test.ts gives: client.ts resolves DUCKDB_DATA_DIR once at module-
+  // evaluation time, so it has to be loaded after the env var is set.
+  const { getConnection, closeConnection } = (await import('../src/db/client.js')) as typeof ClientModule;
+  const seedConnection = await getConnection();
+  await runMigrations(seedConnection);
+  // A cache entry that already holds a researched value, belonging to no
+  // Listing — the "another Listing already paid for this research" state
+  // Req 6/7 are about. Seeded with raw SQL rather than through
+  // partPriceCache.ts so this fixture does not depend on the code the
+  // assertions judge.
+  await seedConnection.run(
+    `INSERT INTO part_price_cache (id, component_category, brand, part_number, estimated_resale_value, last_researched_at)
+     VALUES (gen_random_uuid(), 'GPU', 'AMD', $part_number, 250, current_timestamp)`,
+    { part_number: PRE_CACHED },
+  );
+  // Single-writer rule (client.ts's own module doc): this connection must be
+  // closed before the server process opens the same file.
+  await closeConnection();
+
+  server = await startTestServer(TEST_PORT, {
+    DUCKDB_DATA_DIR: dataDir,
+    IDENTIFY_PARTS_POOL_SIZE: '0',
+    PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE: '0',
+  });
+  const sourceRes = await postJson<SourceResponse>(`${server.baseUrl}/api/sources`, {
+    name: 'Price Concurrency Source',
+    platformType: 'ebay',
+    url: 'https://example.com/price-concurrency',
+    notes: null,
+  });
+  expect(sourceRes.status, `seed Source creation failed: ${JSON.stringify(sourceRes.body)}`).toBe(201);
+  const sourceId = sourceRes.body.id;
+
+  // --- Req 13: two Listing saves racing on one brand-new combo ----------
+  // Promise.all, not two awaits: both POSTs are in flight together.
+  raceTwoSaves = await Promise.all([
+    postJson<ListingDetail>(
+      `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+      listingBody(sourceId, 'Race save A', [part({ componentCategory: 'GPU', brand: 'NVIDIA', partNumber: RACE_TWO_SAVES })]),
+    ),
+    postJson<ListingDetail>(
+      `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+      listingBody(sourceId, 'Race save B', [part({ componentCategory: 'GPU', brand: 'NVIDIA', partNumber: RACE_TWO_SAVES })]),
+    ),
+  ]);
+
+  // --- Req 13: an edit racing a create on one brand-new combo -----------
+  // The Listing the PUT edits starts with a part the pipeline ignores, so
+  // the combo under test is genuinely first encountered during the race.
+  const editTarget = await postJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'Edit race target', [part({ componentCategory: 'Other', description: 'unidentified' })]),
+  );
+  expect(editTarget.status, `edit-race seed failed: ${JSON.stringify(editTarget.body)}`).toBe(201);
+  racePutVsPost = await Promise.all([
+    putJson<ListingDetail>(
+      `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings/${editTarget.body.id}`,
+      listingBody(sourceId, 'Edit race target', [part({ componentCategory: 'CPU', brand: 'Intel', partNumber: RACE_PUT_VS_POST })]),
+    ),
+    postJson<ListingDetail>(
+      `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+      listingBody(sourceId, 'Create racing the edit', [part({ componentCategory: 'CPU', brand: 'Intel', partNumber: RACE_PUT_VS_POST })]),
+    ),
+  ]);
+
+  // --- Req 4, the edit half on its own ----------------------------------
+  // The race above cannot show that a PUT triggers anything: the POST it
+  // races would account for the single cache row and job by itself. This
+  // scenario gives the edit path a combo nothing else ever touches.
+  const editOnlyTarget = await postJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'Edit-only target', [part({ componentCategory: 'Other', description: 'unidentified' })]),
+  );
+  expect(editOnlyTarget.status, `edit-only seed failed: ${JSON.stringify(editOnlyTarget.body)}`).toBe(201);
+  editOnlySaved = await putJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings/${editOnlyTarget.body.id}`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'Edit-only target', [part({ componentCategory: 'PSU', brand: 'Seasonic', partNumber: PUT_ONLY })]),
+  );
+
+  // --- Req 14: resaving an auto row that is still waiting for its price --
+  resaveCreated = await postJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'Resave of an auto row', [part({ componentCategory: 'RAM', brand: 'Corsair', partNumber: RESAVE_AUTO })]),
+  );
+  expect(resaveCreated.status, `resave seed failed: ${JSON.stringify(resaveCreated.body)}`).toBe(201);
+  // The editor round-trips the row's own priceSource when the user has not
+  // touched it, and the price has not arrived yet, so the value is still null.
+  resaveUpdated = await putJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings/${resaveCreated.body.id}`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'Resave of an auto row', [
+      part({ componentCategory: 'RAM', brand: 'Corsair', partNumber: RESAVE_AUTO, priceSource: 'auto' }),
+    ]),
+  );
+
+  // --- Req 14/15: a row the user priced himself, created then resaved ----
+  userPricedCreated = await postJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'User-priced row', [
+      part({ componentCategory: 'Storage', brand: 'Samsung', partNumber: USER_PRICED, estimatedResaleValue: 410 }),
+    ]),
+  );
+  expect(userPricedCreated.status, `user-priced seed failed: ${JSON.stringify(userPricedCreated.body)}`).toBe(201);
+  userPricedUpdated = await putJson<ListingDetail>(
+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings/${userPricedCreated.body.id}`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'User-priced row', [
+      part({
+        componentCategory: 'Storage',
+        brand: 'Samsung',
+        partNumber: USER_PRICED,
+        description: 'Pulled from the tower, lid scuffed',
+        estimatedResaleValue: 410,
+        priceSource: null,
+      }),
+    ]),
+  );
+
+  // --- Req 3: half-identified parts ------------------------------------
+  halfIdentified = await postJson<ListingDetail>(

+      part({ componentCategory: 'PSU', brand: 'Corsair', partNumber: null, description: 'unknown model' }),
+      part({ componentCategory: 'RAM', brand: null, partNumber: HALF_IDENTIFIED, description: 'unknown brand' }),
+    ]),
+  );
+

+  // Two rows of the same combo in one request: one the pipeline may own,
+  // one the user priced himself.

+    `${server.baseUrl}/api/listings`,
+    listingBody(sourceId, 'Cache hit', [
+      part({ componentCategory: 'GPU', brand: 'AMD', partNumber: PRE_CACHED, description: 'pipeline row' }),
+      part({
+        componentCategory: 'GPU',
+        brand: 'AMD',
+        partNumber: PRE_CACHED,
+        description: 'user-priced row',
+        estimatedResaleValue: USER_TYPED_VALUE,
+      }),
+    ]),

+  // asserted is the stored state after the trigger ran.
+  cacheHitListing = await getJson<ListingDetail>(`${server.baseUrl}/api/listings/${cacheHitListing.body.id}`);

+  await server.stop();
+  serverStopped = true;
+  connection = await getConnection();
+}, 180_000);

+afterAll(async () => {
+  if (!serverStopped) {
+    await server.stop();
+  }
+  delete process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR;
+  await rm(dataDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+}, 30_000);

+describe('two Listing saves racing on one brand-new combo (Req 13)', () => {
+  it('both saves succeed and both rows are claimed for the pipeline', () => {
+    for (const res of raceTwoSaves) {
+      expect(res.status, `save failed: ${JSON.stringify(res.body)}`).toBe(201);
+      expect(res.body.expectedParts[0].priceSource).toBe('auto');
+    }
+  });
+
+  it('leaves exactly one cache row, still unpriced (Req 9)', async () => {
+    const rows = await cacheRows(RACE_TWO_SAVES);
+    // Rows dumped verbatim on failure: a duplicate's own column values are
+    // the evidence for which writer produced it (same reason
+    // concurrency.test.ts prints failing bodies rather than a count).
+    expect(rows, `cache rows: ${JSON.stringify(rows)}`).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(rows[0].estimated_resale_value).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('leaves exactly one research job, and no duplicate of it', async () => {
+    const rows = await jobRows(RACE_TWO_SAVES);
+    const nonTerminal = rows.filter((row) => row.stage === 'queued' || row.stage === 'pricing');
+    expect(nonTerminal, `job rows: ${JSON.stringify(rows)}`).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(rows, `job rows: ${JSON.stringify(rows)}`).toHaveLength(1);
+  });
+
+  // Req 5: the research itself never happens inside the request. Both
+  // requests returned, yet the job is untouched at `queued` and the cache
+  // row is unpriced — the save enqueued the work and left.
+  it('returns from the request with the research still merely queued (Req 4, 5)', async () => {
+    const rows = await jobRows(RACE_TWO_SAVES);
+    expect(rows[0].stage).toBe('queued');
+  });
+});
+
+describe('an edit racing a create on one brand-new combo (Req 13)', () => {
+  it('both requests succeed', () => {
+    expect(racePutVsPost[0].status, `PUT failed: ${JSON.stringify(racePutVsPost[0].body)}`).toBe(200);
+    expect(racePutVsPost[1].status, `POST failed: ${JSON.stringify(racePutVsPost[1].body)}`).toBe(201);
+  });
+
+  it('leaves exactly one cache row and one research job', async () => {
+    expect(await cacheRows(RACE_PUT_VS_POST)).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(await jobRows(RACE_PUT_VS_POST)).toHaveLength(1);
+  });
+});
+
+describe('an edit that newly identifies a part (Req 4, 5)', () => {
+  it('claims the row for the pipeline on the edit path', () => {
+    expect(editOnlySaved.status, `edit failed: ${JSON.stringify(editOnlySaved.body)}`).toBe(200);
+    expect(editOnlySaved.body.expectedParts[0].priceSource).toBe('auto');
+  });
+
+  it('inserts the cache row and enqueues the research the edit alone triggered', async () => {
+    const cache = await cacheRows(PUT_ONLY);
+    expect(cache, `cache rows: ${JSON.stringify(cache)}`).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(cache[0].estimated_resale_value).toBeNull();
+    const jobs = await jobRows(PUT_ONLY);
+    expect(jobs, `job rows: ${JSON.stringify(jobs)}`).toHaveLength(1);
+    // Still queued after the request returned: the edit enqueued the
+    // research rather than performing it (Req 5).
+    expect(jobs[0].stage).toBe('queued');
+  });
+});

+describe('resaving an auto row that is still waiting for its price (Req 14)', () => {
+  it('keeps the row claimed by the pipeline across the resave', () => {
+    expect(resaveCreated.body.expectedParts[0].priceSource).toBe('auto');
+    expect(resaveUpdated.status, `resave failed: ${JSON.stringify(resaveUpdated.body)}`).toBe(200);
+    expect(resaveUpdated.body.expectedParts[0].priceSource).toBe('auto');
+  });
+
+  it('does not enqueue a second job for the combo', async () => {
+    expect(await jobRows(RESAVE_AUTO)).toHaveLength(1);
+  });

+    const rows = await cacheRows(RESAVE_AUTO);
+    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(rows[0].estimated_resale_value).toBeNull();
+    expect(rows[0].last_researched_at).toBeNull();
+  });
+});

+describe('a row the user priced himself (Req 14, 15)', () => {
+  it('is never claimed by the pipeline, on create or on resave', () => {
+    expect(userPricedCreated.body.expectedParts[0].priceSource).toBeNull();
+    expect(userPricedUpdated.status, `resave failed: ${JSON.stringify(userPricedUpdated.body)}`).toBe(200);
+    expect(userPricedUpdated.body.expectedParts[0].priceSource).toBeNull();
+    expect(userPricedUpdated.body.expectedParts[0].estimatedResaleValue).toBe(410);
+  });

+  it('never enters the cache and never enqueues research', async () => {
+    expect(await cacheRows(USER_PRICED)).toHaveLength(0);

+  });

+    );

+    expect(Number(counts.cache_count)).toBe(0);
+    expect(Number(counts.job_count)).toBe(0);
+  });

+    expect(await cacheRows(HALF_IDENTIFIED)).toHaveLength(0);
+    expect(await jobRows(HALF_IDENTIFIED)).toHaveLength(0);

+    expect(pipelineRow?.priceSource).toBe('auto');

+  // figure the user typed on his own row of the very same combo survives a
+  // propagation that lands in the same request.
+  it('leaves the user-priced row of that same combo at the user figure', () => {
+    const userRow = cacheHitListing.body.expectedParts.find((p) => p.description === 'user-priced row');
+    expect(userRow?.priceSource).toBeNull();
+    expect(userRow?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(USER_TYPED_VALUE);
+  });
+
+  // Req 7: a cache hit assigns, it does not research. No job was enqueued
+  // for this combo by anyone, so a job row here could only have come from
+  // this save falling through to the miss path.
+  it('enqueues no research job at all for the combo', async () => {
+    expect(await jobRows(PRE_CACHED)).toHaveLength(0);
+  });
+
+  it('leaves the single cache row and its value untouched', async () => {
+    const rows = await cacheRows(PRE_CACHED);
+    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(Number(rows[0].estimated_resale_value)).toBe(PRE_CACHED_VALUE);
+  });

    +// Tests for apps/server/src/db/queries/pricePartsJobs.ts and the
+// price_parts_jobs table DDL in apps/server/src/db/schema.ts
+// (part-price-estimation feature T4). Exercises every exported query
+// function directly against a real isolated DuckDB file — no HTTP server
+// involved, the same direct-DB pattern identifyPartsJobs.test.ts uses for
+// the sibling identify-parts queue.
+import type * as DbModule from '../src/db/index.js';
+import type * as ClientModule from '../src/db/client.js';
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { afterAll, beforeAll, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
+import { runMigrations } from '../src/db/migrate.js';
+
+let dataDir: string;
+let db: typeof DbModule;
+let getConnection: typeof ClientModule.getConnection;
+
+beforeAll(async () => {
+  dataDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'pc-parts-flipper-price-parts-jobs-test-'));
+  process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR = dataDir;
+  // Exception (ts-no-dynamic-import): client.ts's DATA_DIR is computed once,
+  // from DUCKDB_DATA_DIR, at module-evaluation time into a file-scoped const.
+  // A static top-of-file import would evaluate before this beforeAll sets the
+  // env var and would therefore open the shared dev database instead of this
+  // test's isolated temp dir — the dynamic import here is what makes the
+  // module-loading timing land after the env var.
+  db = (await import('../src/db/index.js')) as typeof DbModule;
+  ({ getConnection } = (await import('../src/db/client.js')) as typeof ClientModule);
+  await runMigrations(await getConnection());
+});
+
+afterAll(async () => {
+  delete process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR;
+  await rm(dataDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+});
+
+// claimNextQueuedPriceJob picks the oldest queued job GLOBALLY (it is not
+// scoped to any combo), and this suite shares one DuckDB file across every
+// test. Without draining first, a job a previous test left queued would be
+// the one a later claim returns. Draining via raw SQL — not through the
+// module under test — gives each test a clean slate regardless of run order.
+beforeEach(async () => {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  await connection.run(
+    `UPDATE price_parts_jobs SET stage = 'failed', completed_at = current_timestamp
+     WHERE stage NOT IN ('completed', 'failed')`,
+  );
+});
+
+// Direct row read for one combo, bypassing the query module under test so
+// the assertions below don't depend on the same code they're verifying.
+async function readJobRows(brand: string, partNumber: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>[]> {
+  const connection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT * FROM price_parts_jobs WHERE brand = $brand AND part_number = $part_number ORDER BY created_at`,
+    { brand, part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+}
+
+// Each test uses its own part number so the global claim/drain behaviour of
+// one test can never be mistaken for another test's rows.
+let partCounter = 0;
+function uniquePartNumber(): string {
+  partCounter += 1;
+  return `RTX-TEST-${partCounter}`;
+}
+
+describe('pricePartsJobs — createQueuedPriceJob', () => {
+  it('inserts a queued job carrying the full combo and no timestamps yet', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'NVIDIA', partNumber);
+
+    expect(job.category).toBe('GPU');
+    expect(job.brand).toBe('NVIDIA');
+    expect(job.partNumber).toBe(partNumber);
+    expect(job.stage).toBe('queued');
+    expect(job.errorMessage).toBeNull();
+    expect(job.startedAt).toBeNull();
+    expect(job.completedAt).toBeNull();
+    expect(typeof job.id).toBe('string');
+
+    const rows = await readJobRows('NVIDIA', partNumber);
+    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(rows[0].stage).toBe('queued');
+    expect(rows[0].component_category).toBe('GPU');
+  });
+});
+
+describe('pricePartsJobs — claimNextQueuedPriceJob', () => {
+  it('flips the claimed job to pricing and stamps started_at', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    await db.createQueuedPriceJob('CPU', 'Intel', partNumber);
+
+    const claimed = await db.claimNextQueuedPriceJob();
+
+    expect(claimed?.partNumber).toBe(partNumber);
+    expect(claimed?.stage).toBe('pricing');
+    expect(claimed?.startedAt).not.toBeNull();
+
+    const rows = await readJobRows('Intel', partNumber);
+    expect(rows[0].stage).toBe('pricing');
+    expect(rows[0].started_at).not.toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('claims the oldest queued job first and never hands the same job to two claimers', async () => {
+    const firstPart = uniquePartNumber();
+    const secondPart = uniquePartNumber();
+    // created_at is a timestamp; inserting sequentially (awaited) is what
+    // makes "oldest" well-defined here.
+    await db.createQueuedPriceJob('CPU', 'Intel', firstPart);
+    await db.createQueuedPriceJob('CPU', 'Intel', secondPart);
+
+    const first = await db.claimNextQueuedPriceJob();
+    const second = await db.claimNextQueuedPriceJob();
+
+    expect(first?.partNumber).toBe(firstPart);
+    expect(second?.partNumber).toBe(secondPart);
+    expect(first?.id).not.toBe(second?.id);
+  });
+
+  it('returns null when nothing is queued', async () => {
+    expect(await db.claimNextQueuedPriceJob()).toBeNull();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('pricePartsJobs — stage transitions', () => {
+  it('updatePriceJobStage sets the stage without touching completed_at', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('RAM', 'Corsair', partNumber);
+
+    await db.updatePriceJobStage(job.id, 'pricing');
+
+    const rows = await readJobRows('Corsair', partNumber);
+    expect(rows[0].stage).toBe('pricing');
+    expect(rows[0].completed_at).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('completePriceJob marks the job completed and stamps completed_at', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('RAM', 'Corsair', partNumber);
+
+    await db.completePriceJob(job.id);
+
+    const rows = await readJobRows('Corsair', partNumber);
+    expect(rows[0].stage).toBe('completed');
+    expect(rows[0].completed_at).not.toBeNull();
+    expect(rows[0].error_message).toBeNull();
+  });
+
+  it('failPriceJob marks the job failed, stamps completed_at and stores the message', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('PSU', 'Seasonic', partNumber);
+
+    await db.failPriceJob(job.id, 'omp exited non-zero');
+
+    const rows = await readJobRows('Seasonic', partNumber);
+    expect(rows[0].stage).toBe('failed');
+    expect(rows[0].completed_at).not.toBeNull();
+    expect(rows[0].error_message).toBe('omp exited non-zero');
+  });
+});
+
+describe('pricePartsJobs — hasNonTerminalPriceJob', () => {
+  it('is false for a combo that has never had a job', async () => {
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', uniquePartNumber())).toBe(false);
+  });
+
+  it('is true while a job for the combo is queued, and still true once it is pricing', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber);
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber)).toBe(true);
+
+    await db.updatePriceJobStage(job.id, 'pricing');
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber)).toBe(true);
+  });
+
+  // Both terminal stages must read as "no job in flight" — a suite proving
+  // only `completed` would leave `failed` free to block every future retry
+  // of that combo forever.
+  it('is false once the combo\'s job is completed', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber);
+    await db.completePriceJob(job.id);
+
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber)).toBe(false);
+  });
+
+  it('is false once the combo\'s job has failed', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    const job = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber);
+    await db.failPriceJob(job.id, 'no confident answer');
+
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber)).toBe(false);
+  });
+
+  // The lookup is keyed on all three columns; a match on brand+partNumber
+  // alone (or on brand alone) would wrongly suppress a real enqueue.
+  it('does not match a different category, brand, or part number', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+    await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', partNumber);
+
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('CPU', 'AMD', partNumber)).toBe(false);
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'NVIDIA', partNumber)).toBe(false);
+    expect(await db.hasNonTerminalPriceJob('GPU', 'AMD', `${partNumber}-other`)).toBe(false);
+  });
+});
+
+describe('pricePartsJobs — resetOrphanedPriceJobsToQueued', () => {
+  // Mirrors identifyPartsJobs.test.ts's own resetOrphanedJobsToQueued test:
+  // one job in each of the four stages this function must tell apart, a
+  // real call through the query module (no mocking), and an assertion that
+  // only the orphaned 'pricing' job moved while the other three rows are
+  // byte-for-byte unchanged. claimNextQueuedPriceJob claims the GLOBALLY
+  // oldest queued job, so the pricing job is created and claimed first,
+  // before the queued job is created — otherwise the claim below would
+  // grab the wrong row.
+  it('resets only the pricing job to queued and clears started_at, leaving queued/completed/failed jobs completely untouched', async () => {
+    const pricingPart = uniquePartNumber();
+    const pricingJobCreated = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'EVGA', pricingPart);
+    const pricingJob = await db.claimNextQueuedPriceJob();
+    expect(pricingJob?.id).toBe(pricingJobCreated.id);
+    expect(pricingJob?.stage).toBe('pricing');
+
+    const queuedPart = uniquePartNumber();
+    const queuedJob = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'EVGA', queuedPart);
+
+    const completedPart = uniquePartNumber();
+    const completedJob = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'EVGA', completedPart);
+    await db.completePriceJob(completedJob.id);
+
+    const failedPart = uniquePartNumber();
+    const failedJob = await db.createQueuedPriceJob('GPU', 'EVGA', failedPart);
+    await db.failPriceJob(failedJob.id, 'no confident answer');
+
+    const beforeQueuedRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', queuedPart);
+    const beforeCompletedRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', completedPart);
+    const beforeFailedRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', failedPart);
+
+    const resetIds = await db.resetOrphanedPriceJobsToQueued();
+
+    expect(resetIds).toEqual([pricingJob!.id]);
+
+    const afterPricingRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', pricingPart);
+    expect(afterPricingRows).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(afterPricingRows[0].stage).toBe('queued');
+    expect(afterPricingRows[0].started_at).toBeNull();
+
+    // The other three rows must be completely untouched, not merely
+    // "still the right stage" — comparing the full raw row (including
+    // started_at/completed_at/updated_at) catches a WHERE clause broad
+    // enough to match them but narrow enough to leave stage alone.
+    const afterQueuedRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', queuedPart);
+    expect(afterQueuedRows).toEqual(beforeQueuedRows);
+    expect(afterQueuedRows[0].stage).toBe('queued');
+    expect(afterQueuedRows[0].id).toBe(queuedJob.id);
+
+    const afterCompletedRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', completedPart);
+    expect(afterCompletedRows).toEqual(beforeCompletedRows);
+    expect(afterCompletedRows[0].stage).toBe('completed');
+
+    const afterFailedRows = await readJobRows('EVGA', failedPart);
+    expect(afterFailedRows).toEqual(beforeFailedRows);
+    expect(afterFailedRows[0].stage).toBe('failed');
+  });
+});

    +// T4 proof: apps/server/src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.ts (Req 5-7, 13-14) —
+// the cache-hit/miss decision at nodes B-G of the feature's architecture
+// diagram.
+//
+// This suite isolates the trigger from DuckDB by mocking the db/index.js
+// barrel it imports, the same module-under-test isolation
+// identifyPartsWorkerPool.test.ts uses for the sibling worker pool. What is
+// under test here is purely the decision — which of propagate / insert /
+// enqueue happens for which cache state — and the query functions it calls
+// already have their own direct-DB suites (partPriceCache.test.ts,
+// pricePartsJobs.test.ts).
+import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
+import type { ComponentCategory, ExpectedPart, PartPriceCacheEntry } from '../src/db/types.js';
+import { logger } from '../src/logger.js';
+
+const findPriceCacheEntry = vi.fn<[ComponentCategory, string, string], Promise<PartPriceCacheEntry | null>>();
+const insertPriceCacheEntry = vi.fn<[ComponentCategory, string, string], Promise<{ inserted: boolean }>>();
+const propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts = vi.fn<[ComponentCategory, string, string, number], Promise<void>>();
+const createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent = vi.fn<[ComponentCategory, string, string], Promise<{ inserted: boolean }>>();
+
+// Mocking the whole db/index.js barrel (rather than the two query files)
+// matches how the trigger itself imports it, so vi.mock's specifier resolves
+// to the exact module the trigger sees. vi.mock is hoisted above this file's
+// imports, so the static import of pricePartsTrigger.js below already sees
+// these mocks.
+vi.mock('../src/db/index.js', () => ({
+  findPriceCacheEntry: (...args: [ComponentCategory, string, string]) => findPriceCacheEntry(...args),
+  insertPriceCacheEntry: (...args: [ComponentCategory, string, string]) => insertPriceCacheEntry(...args),
+  propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts: (...args: [ComponentCategory, string, string, number]) =>
+    propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts(...args),
+  createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent: (...args: [ComponentCategory, string, string]) =>
+    createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent(...args),
+}));
+
+import { triggerPriceLookups } from '../src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js';
+
+/** Builds an Expected Part fixture; only the four fields the trigger reads matter. */
+function partFixture(overrides: Partial<ExpectedPart> = {}): ExpectedPart {
+  return {
+    id: 'part-1',
+    listingId: 'listing-1',
+    componentCategory: 'GPU',
+    brand: 'NVIDIA',
+    partNumber: 'RTX 3060',
+    description: 'Pulled from the tower',
+    estimatedResaleValue: null,
+    identityCandidates: [],
+    aiConfidence: 'high',
+    aiSource: 'text',
+    priceSource: 'auto',
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    ...overrides,
+  };
+}
+
+function cacheFixture(estimatedResaleValue: number | null): PartPriceCacheEntry {
+  return {
+    id: 'cache-1',
+    category: 'GPU',
+    brand: 'NVIDIA',
+    partNumber: 'RTX 3060',
+    estimatedResaleValue,
+    lastResearchedAt: estimatedResaleValue === null ? null : '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+  };
+}
+
+beforeEach(() => {
+  vi.clearAllMocks();
+  findPriceCacheEntry.mockResolvedValue(null);
+  insertPriceCacheEntry.mockResolvedValue({ inserted: true });
+  propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts.mockResolvedValue(undefined);
+  createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent.mockResolvedValue({ inserted: true });
+});
+
+afterEach(() => {
+  vi.restoreAllMocks();
+});
+
+describe('triggerPriceLookups — cache hit with a value (diagram node D)', () => {
+  it('writes the cached value onto matching rows and enqueues nothing', async () => {
+    findPriceCacheEntry.mockResolvedValue(cacheFixture(275));
+
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture()]);
+
+    expect(propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts).toHaveBeenCalledWith('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3060', 275);
+    expect(insertPriceCacheEntry).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  // A zero is a real researched value, not an absence — a truthiness check
+  // on the cached value would silently drop it back into the "still null"
+  // branch and leave the row unpriced forever.
+  it('propagates a cached value of 0 rather than treating it as absent', async () => {
+    findPriceCacheEntry.mockResolvedValue(cacheFixture(0));
+
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture()]);
+
+    expect(propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts).toHaveBeenCalledWith('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3060', 0);
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('triggerPriceLookups — cache hit still unpriced (diagram node E)', () => {
+  it('does nothing further: no propagation, no insert, no second job', async () => {
+    findPriceCacheEntry.mockResolvedValue(cacheFixture(null));
+
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture()]);
+
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+    expect(insertPriceCacheEntry).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('triggerPriceLookups — cache miss (diagram nodes F-G)', () => {
+  it('inserts the cache row and atomically enqueues a research job', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture()]);
+
+    expect(insertPriceCacheEntry).toHaveBeenCalledWith('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3060');
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledWith('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3060');
+    expect(propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  // Req 13, race loser: another writer won the cache-insert race, so this
+  // writer's insert reports inserted: false. The winner may not have
+  // reached its own enqueue yet, so "I lost the race" is not evidence a job
+  // exists — the atomic enqueue-unless-non-terminal call is still made, and
+  // the DB's own WHERE NOT EXISTS is what actually answers whether a job
+  // already exists (pricePartsJobs.test.ts, pricePartsTriggerRace.test.ts).
+  it('still calls the atomic enqueue when it lost the insert race', async () => {
+    insertPriceCacheEntry.mockResolvedValue({ inserted: false });
+
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture()]);
+
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledWith('GPU', 'NVIDIA', 'RTX 3060');
+  });
+
+  // Req 13, the duplicate guard's outcome at this layer: the atomic
+  // enqueue reporting inserted: false (a job was already queued/pricing,
+  // per the DB's own WHERE NOT EXISTS) ends resolveCombo with no further
+  // action — nothing else is called off the back of that result.
+  it('takes no further action when the atomic enqueue reports a duplicate was skipped', async () => {
+    createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent.mockResolvedValue({ inserted: false });
+
+    await expect(triggerPriceLookups([partFixture()])).resolves.toBeUndefined();
+
+    expect(insertPriceCacheEntry).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+});
+
+describe('triggerPriceLookups — eligibility (diagram node B)', () => {
+  it('skips a part with no brand', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture({ brand: null })]);
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('skips a part with no part number', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture({ partNumber: null })]);
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  // Req 14: a row that already carries a price is never re-priced by the
+  // pipeline, whoever put that figure there.
+  it('skips a part that already has an estimated resale value', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture({ estimatedResaleValue: 120 })]);
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('skips a part priced at 0, which is a real price and not an absent one', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture({ estimatedResaleValue: 0 })]);
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+
+  it('processes only the eligible parts out of a mixed set', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([
+      partFixture({ id: 'a', brand: null }),
+      partFixture({ id: 'b', componentCategory: 'CPU', brand: 'Intel', partNumber: 'i7-9700K' }),
+      partFixture({ id: 'c', estimatedResaleValue: 50 }),
+    ]);
+
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).toHaveBeenCalledWith('CPU', 'Intel', 'i7-9700K');
+  });
+});
+
+describe('triggerPriceLookups — de-duplication and failure isolation', () => {
+  // Two rows of the same part in one Listing are one combo. Resolving it
+  // twice would race the trigger against itself over the same cache row and
+  // job queue, which is the very duplicate this module exists to prevent.
+  it('resolves a combo once even when several parts share it', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([partFixture({ id: 'a' }), partFixture({ id: 'b' })]);
+
+    expect(findPriceCacheEntry).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+  });
+
+  it('treats the same brand/part number under different categories as different combos', async () => {
+    await triggerPriceLookups([
+      partFixture({ id: 'a', componentCategory: 'GPU' }),
+      partFixture({ id: 'b', componentCategory: 'Other' }),
+    ]);
+
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);
+  });
+
+  // Best-effort background trigger: the write it hangs off already
+  // succeeded, so one combo blowing up must neither reject nor stop the
+  // rest — the same contract promoteReferencesForExpectedParts holds.
+  it('logs and continues when one combo fails, still resolving the others', async () => {
+    const errorSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'error').mockImplementation(() => logger);
+    findPriceCacheEntry.mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('connection lost'));
+
+    await expect(
+      triggerPriceLookups([
+        partFixture({ id: 'a' }),
+        partFixture({ id: 'b', componentCategory: 'CPU', brand: 'Intel', partNumber: 'i7-9700K' }),
+      ]),
+    ).resolves.toBeUndefined();
+
+    expect(errorSpy).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJobIfAbsent).toHaveBeenCalledWith('CPU', 'Intel', 'i7-9700K');
+  });
+});

    +// TE12 proof: `triggerPriceLookups` (apps/server/src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.ts)
+// driven against a REAL, unmocked DuckDB file — unlike pricePartsTrigger.test.ts,
+// which mocks the db/index.js barrel to isolate the decision logic, this suite
+// exists specifically to prove the real SQL in
+// propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts (apps/server/src/db/queries/partPriceCache.ts)
+// actually enforces the `price_source = 'auto'` guard end to end, through the
+// trigger's own public entry point rather than by calling that query function
+// directly.
+//
+// Scenario (R12's own finding, and A22/A23's three-state price_source model):
+// two Expected Parts on two DIFFERENT Listings share one
+// (category, brand, partNumber) combo. One row (`price_source: 'manual'`) is
+// a user who explicitly cleared a previously auto-priced value — per A22
+// this is a permanent, distinct claim, not the same as null's "undecided".
+// The other row (`price_source: 'auto'`) is still pipeline-owned. Both rows
+// have a null estimatedResaleValue, so both pass triggerPriceLookups' own
+// node-B eligibility filter (brand + partNumber present, value still null —
+// that filter does not itself look at price_source at all) and both resolve
+// to the same combo. A cached, non-null price for that combo must propagate
+// onto the 'auto' row and must never reach the 'manual' row — proving the
+// manual row's protection survives even after re-entering the trigger.
+import type * as DbModule from '../src/db/index.js';
+import type * as ClientModule from '../src/db/client.js';
+import type * as TriggerModule from '../src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js';
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
+import { runMigrations } from '../src/db/migrate.js';
+
+const CATEGORY = 'GPU';
+const BRAND = 'NVIDIA';
+const PART_NUMBER = 'RTX 4090 Cross-Listing';
+const CACHED_VALUE = 777;
+
+let dataDir: string;
+let db: typeof DbModule;
+let getConnection: typeof ClientModule.getConnection;
+let triggerPriceLookups: typeof TriggerModule.triggerPriceLookups;
+
+beforeAll(async () => {
+  dataDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'pc-parts-flipper-price-cross-listing-test-'));
+  process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR = dataDir;
+  // Exception (ts-no-dynamic-import): same timing constraint as
+  // partPriceCache.test.ts's own beforeAll — client.ts's DATA_DIR constant
+  // is computed once, from DUCKDB_DATA_DIR, at module-evaluation time, for
+  // every module in this chain (db/index.js, db/client.js, and
+  // jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js, which imports db/index.js itself). A
+  // static top-of-file import of any of the three would evaluate before
+  // this beforeAll sets the env var and would open the shared dev database
+  // instead of this test's isolated temp dir.
+  db = (await import('../src/db/index.js')) as typeof DbModule;
+  ({ getConnection } = (await import('../src/db/client.js')) as typeof ClientModule);
+  ({ triggerPriceLookups } = (await import('../src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js')) as typeof TriggerModule);
+  await runMigrations(await getConnection());
+});
+
+afterAll(async () => {
+  delete process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR;
+  await rm(dataDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+});
+
+/** Creates one Source + Listing + Expected Part, with the given price_source set directly on insert (the three-state field now accepts it as a first-class CreateExpectedPartInput value — no post-insert UPDATE needed). */
+async function seedPart(priceSource: 'auto' | 'manual' | null): Promise<string> {
+  const source = await db.createSource({

+    platformType: 'other',

+    notes: null,
+  });

+    title: `Cross-listing test (${priceSource ?? 'undecided'})`,
+    description: null,

+    listingDate: new Date().toISOString(),
+    acquisitionFees: 0,

+    externalItemId: null,
+  });

+    componentCategory: CATEGORY,
+    brand: BRAND,
+    partNumber: PART_NUMBER,
+    description: 'Cross-listing test part',
+    estimatedResaleValue: null,
+    identityCandidates: [],
+    aiConfidence: null,
+    aiSource: null,
+    priceSource,
+  });
+  return part.id;
+}

+    const autoPart = await db.getExpectedPartById(autoPartId);
+    expect(autoPart?.estimatedResaleValue).toBe(CACHED_VALUE);
+  });

    +// T4 standing-check proof, in-process half: two genuinely racing writers
+// hitting the SAME (component_category, brand, part_number) for the first
+// time never leave two cache rows, two duplicate background jobs, or a
+// crash behind (Req 13).
+//
+// Unlike pricePartsTrigger.test.ts — which mocks the db/index.js barrel and
+// therefore proves only the decision shape, with every "await" resolving
+// instantly and no real interleaving — this suite runs the REAL
+// jobs/pricePartsTrigger.ts against a REAL isolated DuckDB file and fires
+// the racing calls through Promise.all, the same genuine-concurrency
+// pattern concurrency.test.ts and identifyPartsConcurrency.test.ts use at
+// the HTTP layer. The point is the interleaving: resolveCombo's cache read,
+// insert, job check and enqueue are four separate statements, so two
+// writers that enter it together can each observe the pre-race state and
+// both act on it.
+//
+// The writers modelled here are the two Listing saves Req 13 names first.
+// Req 13's other pair — a save landing on top of the daily refresh sweep —
+// is deliberately NOT a test in this file: the save path only ever reaches
+// its enqueue on a cache MISS, and the sweep only ever visits combos that
+// are already IN the cache, so any test that sets both up far enough to run
+// them together leaves the save short-circuiting at "the row already
+// exists, research is someone else's" and never reaching the gate the test
+// would claim to be guarding. One was written here, and a mutation that
+// deleted the sweep's own dedup gate outright did not make it fail
+// (SURVIVED) — it asserted nothing. The window in which that pair really
+// can collide (the sweep's snapshot catching a row between a save's insert
+// and that same save's enqueue) is the same unguarded check-then-act the
+// two tests below already expose deterministically. The HTTP-level variant
+// — two real concurrent POST /api/listings requests — lives in
+// pricePartsConcurrency.test.ts.
+import type * as ClientModule from '../src/db/client.js';
+import type * as TriggerModule from '../src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js';
+import type { DuckDBConnection } from '@duckdb/node-api';
+import type { ExpectedPart } from '../src/db/types.js';
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
+import { runMigrations } from '../src/db/migrate.js';
+
+let dataDir: string;
+let getConnection: typeof ClientModule.getConnection;
+let triggerPriceLookups: typeof TriggerModule.triggerPriceLookups;
+
+beforeAll(async () => {
+  dataDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'pc-parts-flipper-price-trigger-race-'));
+  process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR = dataDir;
+  // Exception (ts-no-dynamic-import), same reason pricePartsJobs.test.ts
+  // gives: client.ts resolves DUCKDB_DATA_DIR once at module-evaluation
+  // time, so every module that transitively imports it has to be loaded
+  // after the env var above is set — including the trigger, which reaches
+  // the database through that same singleton.
+  ({ getConnection } = (await import('../src/db/client.js')) as typeof ClientModule);
+  await runMigrations(await getConnection());
+  ({ triggerPriceLookups } = (await import('../src/jobs/pricePartsTrigger.js')) as typeof TriggerModule);
+}, 60_000);
+
+afterAll(async () => {
+  delete process.env.DUCKDB_DATA_DIR;
+  await rm(dataDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+});
+
+// Each test owns a distinct part number so one test's cache row and jobs
+// can never be counted as another's — the counts below are the whole proof.
+let partCounter = 0;
+function uniquePartNumber(): string {
+  partCounter += 1;
+  return `RACE-${partCounter}`;
+}
+
+/** An Expected Part as the write paths hand it to the trigger; only category/brand/partNumber/estimatedResaleValue are read. */
+function partFixture(partNumber: string, overrides: Partial<ExpectedPart> = {}): ExpectedPart {
+  return {
+    id: `part-${partNumber}`,
+    listingId: `listing-${partNumber}`,
+    componentCategory: 'GPU',
+    brand: 'NVIDIA',
+    partNumber,
+    description: 'Pulled from the tower',
+    estimatedResaleValue: null,
+    identityCandidates: [],
+    aiConfidence: 'high',
+    aiSource: 'text',
+    priceSource: 'auto',
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    ...overrides,
+  };
+}
+
+// Raw row reads, deliberately not going through the query modules the code
+// under test uses, so a bug in those can't hide itself from these counts.
+async function cacheRows(partNumber: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>[]> {
+  const connection: DuckDBConnection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT id, estimated_resale_value FROM part_price_cache WHERE part_number = $part_number`,
+    { part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+}
+
+async function jobRows(partNumber: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>[]> {
+  const connection: DuckDBConnection = await getConnection();
+  const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(
+    `SELECT id, stage FROM price_parts_jobs WHERE part_number = $part_number`,
+    { part_number: partNumber },
+  );
+  return reader.getRowObjectsJson();
+}
+
+describe('triggerPriceLookups under genuine concurrency (Req 13)', () => {
+  it('two racing first encounters of one combo leave exactly one cache row and one job', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+
+    // Promise.all, not two awaits in sequence: both calls are started before
+    // either has finished its cache read, which is the only state in which
+    // the duplicate this test rules out can be produced at all.
+    await Promise.all([
+      triggerPriceLookups([partFixture(partNumber, { id: 'save-a', listingId: 'listing-a' })]),
+      triggerPriceLookups([partFixture(partNumber, { id: 'save-b', listingId: 'listing-b' })]),
+    ]);
+
+    expect(await cacheRows(partNumber)).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(await jobRows(partNumber)).toHaveLength(1);
+  }, 30_000);
+
+  it('eight racing first encounters of one combo still leave exactly one cache row and one job', async () => {
+    const partNumber = uniquePartNumber();
+
+    await Promise.all(
+      Array.from({ length: 8 }, (_unused, i) =>
+        triggerPriceLookups([partFixture(partNumber, { id: `save-${i}`, listingId: `listing-${i}` })]),
+      ),
+    );
+
+    expect(await cacheRows(partNumber)).toHaveLength(1);
+    expect(await jobRows(partNumber)).toHaveLength(1);
+  }, 30_000);
+});

    +// T5 proof: apps/server/src/jobs/pricePartsWorkerPool.ts (Req 11).
+//
+// This suite isolates the worker pool from both DuckDB and the real AI

+// Every pool handle started by a test is stopped in afterEach, so a test's
+// 1000ms poll interval never outlives the test and leaks into the next one

+let activeHandle: { stop(): void } | null = null;

+beforeEach(() => {
+  claimNextQueuedPriceJob.mockReset().mockResolvedValue(null);
+  completePriceJob.mockReset().mockResolvedValue(undefined);
+  failPriceJob.mockReset().mockResolvedValue(undefined);

+    expect(completePriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('job-A');
+    expect(failPriceJob).not.toHaveBeenCalled();

+  // completion, job-2 would never be claimed and ompSourcingRunner would be

+    activeHandle = startPricePartsWorkerPool();
+    await vi.waitFor(() => expect(claimNextQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalled());

+    activeHandle.stop();
+
+    expect(clearIntervalSpy).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+  });

+// T5 fallback proof, mirroring identifyPartsWorkerPool.test.ts's own
+// parsePoolSize coverage for IDENTIFY_PARTS_POOL_SIZE: an empty-string or

+    process.env.PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE = 'not-a-number';

    +// T6 proof: apps/server/src/jobs/priceRefreshScheduler.ts (Req 8).
+//
+// A real 3am cron fire can't practically be asserted, so this suite instead
+// mirrors scan/scheduler.test.ts's own precedent exactly:
+//   (a) unit-tests runScheduledPriceRefresh — the function the cron callback
+//       invokes — directly, against a mocked db/index.ts module (the only
+//       boundary this file crosses; no DuckDB connection is opened); and
+//   (b) confirms startPriceRefreshScheduler registers a valid once-daily
+//       cron expression by asserting on node-cron's own `validate` export
+//       against the literal expression startPriceRefreshScheduler passes to
+//       `schedule`.
+import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi, type MockInstance } from 'vitest';
+import { validate } from 'node-cron';
+import type { ScheduledTask } from 'node-cron';
+import type * as NodeCron from 'node-cron';
+import { logger } from '../src/logger.js';
+import type { PartPriceCacheEntry } from '../src/db/types.js';
+
+// listAllPriceCacheEntries/hasNonTerminalPriceJob/createQueuedPriceJob are
+// the only db/index.ts exports priceRefreshScheduler.ts calls; mocking the
+// whole module keeps this suite from touching DuckDB, the same boundary
+// scheduler.test.ts already exercises on scanService.ts's own behalf.
+// vi.mock is hoisted above this file's imports, so the static import of
+// priceRefreshScheduler.ts below already sees the mocked db/index.js.
+const listAllPriceCacheEntries = vi.fn<[], Promise<PartPriceCacheEntry[]>>();
+const hasNonTerminalPriceJob = vi.fn<[string, string, string], Promise<boolean>>();
+const createQueuedPriceJob = vi.fn<[string, string, string], Promise<unknown>>();
+vi.mock('../src/db/index.js', () => ({
+  listAllPriceCacheEntries: (...args: unknown[]) => listAllPriceCacheEntries(...(args as [])),
+  hasNonTerminalPriceJob: (...args: unknown[]) => hasNonTerminalPriceJob(...(args as [string, string, string])),
+  createQueuedPriceJob: (...args: unknown[]) => createQueuedPriceJob(...(args as [string, string, string])),
+}));
+
+// node-cron's named exports live on a non-configurable ESM namespace object,
+// so `schedule` can't be vi.spyOn'd directly; mocking the module and
+// keeping every other export (validate, in particular) wired to the real
+// implementation is the supported way to observe `schedule`'s call args
+// instead (same pattern as scheduler.test.ts).
+const scheduleMock = vi.fn<(expression: string, fn: () => void) => ScheduledTask>();
+vi.mock('node-cron', async (importOriginal) => {
+  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof NodeCron>();
+  return { ...actual, schedule: (...args: Parameters<typeof scheduleMock>) => scheduleMock(...args) };
+});
+
+// Imported after the vi.mock calls above (hoisted ahead of them regardless)
+// so every call inside priceRefreshScheduler.ts to these db functions
+// resolves to the mocks rather than a real DB-backed implementation.
+import { runScheduledPriceRefresh, startPriceRefreshScheduler } from '../src/jobs/priceRefreshScheduler.js';
+
+let infoSpy: MockInstance<(...args: unknown[]) => void>;
+let errorSpy: MockInstance<(...args: unknown[]) => void>;
+
+function makeEntry(overrides: Partial<PartPriceCacheEntry>): PartPriceCacheEntry {
+  return {
+    id: 'entry-id',
+    category: 'CPU',
+    brand: 'Intel',
+    partNumber: 'i7-9700K',
+    estimatedResaleValue: null,
+    lastResearchedAt: null,
+    createdAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    updatedAt: '2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z',
+    ...overrides,
+  };
+}
+
+beforeEach(() => {
+  listAllPriceCacheEntries.mockReset();
+  hasNonTerminalPriceJob.mockReset();
+  createQueuedPriceJob.mockReset();
+  scheduleMock.mockReset();
+  infoSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'info').mockImplementation(() => logger);
+  errorSpy = vi.spyOn(logger, 'error').mockImplementation(() => logger);
+});
+
+afterEach(() => {
+  vi.restoreAllMocks();
+});

+describe('runScheduledPriceRefresh (T6, Req 8)', () => {
+  it('enqueues a job for every cache entry, including one that already has a non-null value', async () => {
+    const pricedEntry = makeEntry({
+      category: 'CPU',
+      brand: 'Intel',
+      partNumber: 'i7-9700K',
+      estimatedResaleValue: 150,
+    });
+    const unpricedEntry = makeEntry({
+      category: 'GPU',
+      brand: 'Nvidia',
+      partNumber: 'RTX-3080',
+      estimatedResaleValue: null,
+    });
+    listAllPriceCacheEntries.mockResolvedValue([pricedEntry, unpricedEntry]);
+    hasNonTerminalPriceJob.mockResolvedValue(false);
+    createQueuedPriceJob.mockResolvedValue(undefined);
+
+    await expect(runScheduledPriceRefresh()).resolves.toBeUndefined();
+
+    // Req 8: the already-priced entry is swept exactly like the unpriced
+    // one — no filter on estimatedResaleValue anywhere in the sweep.
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('CPU', 'Intel', 'i7-9700K');
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('GPU', 'Nvidia', 'RTX-3080');
+    expect(infoSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
+      expect.objectContaining({ enqueuedCount: 2, skippedCount: 0, totalCount: 2 }),
+      'Scheduled price refresh completed',
+    );
+  });
+
+  it('skips an entry that already has a non-terminal job in flight, enqueuing nothing for it', async () => {
+    const queuedEntry = makeEntry({ category: 'CPU', brand: 'AMD', partNumber: 'R7-5800X' });
+    const freeEntry = makeEntry({ category: 'Motherboard', brand: 'Asus', partNumber: 'ROG-B550' });
+    listAllPriceCacheEntries.mockResolvedValue([queuedEntry, freeEntry]);
+    // Only the first combo already has a non-terminal job; the second does not.
+    hasNonTerminalPriceJob.mockImplementation((category) => Promise.resolve(category === 'CPU'));
+    createQueuedPriceJob.mockResolvedValue(undefined);
+
+    await expect(runScheduledPriceRefresh()).resolves.toBeUndefined();
+
+    // Dedup: the already-queued combo gets no second job...
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).not.toHaveBeenCalledWith('CPU', 'AMD', 'R7-5800X');
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    // ...while the free combo is still enqueued normally.
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('Motherboard', 'Asus', 'ROG-B550');
+    expect(infoSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
+      expect.objectContaining({ enqueuedCount: 1, skippedCount: 1, totalCount: 2 }),
+      'Scheduled price refresh completed',
+    );
+  });
+
+  it('isolates one entry failing to enqueue so later entries still get processed (per-entry isolation, mirrors scanAllEligibleSources)', async () => {
+    const failingEntry = makeEntry({ category: 'CPU', brand: 'AMD', partNumber: 'R7-5800X' });
+    const laterEntry = makeEntry({ category: 'Motherboard', brand: 'Asus', partNumber: 'ROG-B550' });
+    listAllPriceCacheEntries.mockResolvedValue([failingEntry, laterEntry]);
+    hasNonTerminalPriceJob.mockResolvedValue(false);
+    createQueuedPriceJob.mockImplementation((category: string) => {
+      if (category === 'CPU') {
+        return Promise.reject(new Error('enqueue failed for CPU combo'));
+      }
+      return Promise.resolve(undefined);
+    });
+
+    await expect(runScheduledPriceRefresh()).resolves.toBeUndefined();
+
+    // The failing entry's own enqueue attempt was made (and rejected)...
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('CPU', 'AMD', 'R7-5800X');
+    // ...but the entry after it was still reached and enqueued normally,
+    // proving the throw on entry N did not abort the sweep before N+1.
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledWith('Motherboard', 'Asus', 'ROG-B550');
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(2);
+    expect(errorSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
+      expect.objectContaining({
+        category: 'CPU',
+        brand: 'AMD',
+        partNumber: 'R7-5800X',
+        err: 'enqueue failed for CPU combo',
+      }),
+      'Scheduled price refresh failed for one cache entry; continuing with the rest',
+    );
+    expect(infoSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
+      expect.objectContaining({ enqueuedCount: 1, skippedCount: 0, erroredCount: 1, totalCount: 2 }),
+      'Scheduled price refresh completed',
+    );
+  });
+
+  it('catches a rejection of listAllPriceCacheEntries itself, logs it at Error level, and does not throw', async () => {
+    listAllPriceCacheEntries.mockRejectedValue(new Error('DB connection lost'));
+
+    await expect(runScheduledPriceRefresh()).resolves.toBeUndefined();
+
+    expect(errorSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
+      expect.objectContaining({ err: 'DB connection lost' }),
+      'Scheduled price refresh run failed unexpectedly',
+    );
+    expect(createQueuedPriceJob).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
+  });
+});

+describe('startPriceRefreshScheduler (T6, Req 8)', () => {
+  function fakeScheduledTask(): ScheduledTask {
+    return {
+      id: 'fake',
+      start: vi.fn(),
+      stop: vi.fn(),
+      getStatus: vi.fn(() => 'idle'),

+      isBusy: vi.fn(() => false),

+    expect(minute).not.toBe('*');
+    expect(hour).not.toBe('*');
+    expect(dayOfMonth).toBe('*');
+    expect(month).toBe('*');

+    listAllPriceCacheEntries.mockResolvedValue([]);
+    scheduleMock.mockReturnValue(fakeScheduledTask());
+
+    startPriceRefreshScheduler();
+

+    await callback();

+    expect(listAllPriceCacheEntries).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
+    expect(infoSpy).toHaveBeenCalledWith('Scheduled price refresh starting');
+  });

    +// T13 proof for assumption A23's cross-Listing claim (Req 6, 7, 14, 15):
+// a user-owned ('manual') Expected Part with no price of its own still
+// seeds and refreshes the *shared* part_price_cache entry for its combo —
+// that work benefits every other Listing holding the same part — while
+// `propagateCachedPriceToExpectedParts`'s `WHERE price_source = 'auto'`
+// keeps the researched figure off the user's own row forever.
+//
+// A23's two halves are proven separately, because they can fail
+// independently:
+//   1. saving Listing A (one 'manual', unpriced row) leaves a cache row
+//      *and* a queued research job behind for the combo — if
+//      pricePartsTrigger's eligibility filter ever started skipping
+//      'manual' rows, the combo would go unresearched for everyone;
+//   2. once that combo has a price, Listing B's 'auto' row for the same
+//      combo receives it on save while Listing A's 'manual' row stays null
+//      — if the propagation's price_source clause ever went away, the
+//      user's deliberate deletion would be silently undone.
+//
+// Runs against the real server (helpers.ts's spawn-based fixture, the same
+// one priceSourceRoute.test.ts uses) so what is proven is the whole chain:
+// request validation, the route's own 'auto'/'manual' decision, the
+// trigger, the cache row, the job queue and the propagating UPDATE.
+//
+// IDENTIFY_PARTS_POOL_SIZE=0 and PRICE_PARTS_POOL_SIZE=0 keep both worker
+// pools from claiming anything and invoking the real `omp` binary, so the
+// job Listing A enqueues simply sits queued and the "research completed"
+// step is simulated with raw SQL instead — a real AI call has no place in
+// a deterministic test.
+import type { CreateListingInput, ListingDetail } from '@pc-parts-flipper/shared';
+import type * as ClientModule from '../src/db/client.js';
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
+import { runMigrations } from '../src/db/migrate.js';
+import { getJson, postJson, startTestServer, type TestServer } from './helpers.js';
+
+// Unique port so this suite can run alongside every other server-spawning
+// suite (highest currently in use is pricePartsConcurrency.test.ts's
+// 34_641) without a collision.
+const TEST_PORT = 34_650;
+
+// The single (category, brand, part number) combo both Listings share.
+const SHARED_CATEGORY = 'GPU';
+const SHARED_BRAND = 'EVGA';
+const SHARED_PART = 'XLC-CROSS-LISTING';
+
+interface SourceResponse {
+  id: string;
+}

+function listingBody(sourceId: string, title: string, expectedParts: CreateListingInput['expectedParts']): CreateListingInput {
+  return {
+    sourceId,
+    title,
+    description: null,

+    askingPrice: 250,
+    listingDate: '2026-03-01',
+    acquisitionFees: 0,
+    expectedParts,
+  };
+}
